He forbade us gold rings—or wearing gold rings—drinking from silver vessels, saddlecloths, Qassī fabric, and wearing silk, heavy brocade, and silk brocade.”

In the Sahīh, Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Help your brother, whether he is an oppressor or oppressed.”

I said, “O Messenger of Allah, I can help him when he is oppressed, but how do I help him when he is an oppressor?”

He replied, “Prevent him from committing oppression; that is how you help him.”

Moreover, this relieves the distress of the person who has been wronged. In Sahīh Muslim, it is narrated from Abū

---

## Page 9567

Hurayrah that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Whoever relieves a believer of one of the distresses of this world, Allah will relieve him of one of the distresses of the Day of Resurrection. Whoever makes things easier for someone in hardship, Allah will make things easier for him in this world and the Hereafter. Whoever conceals a Muslim’s faults, Allah will conceal his faults in this world and the Hereafter. Allah helps His servant so long as the servant helps his brother.”

Also in Sahīh Muslim, Jābir reported:

“When the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, was asked about healing recitations, he said, ‘Whoever among you can benefit his brother, let him do so.’”

Such help must, however, be rendered justly, as Allah and His Messenger have commanded—for example, through prescribed supplications and remembrances, or by commanding and forbidding the jinn just as one commands and forbids a human. Whatever would be permissible toward a human is likewise permissible here, as when it becomes necessary to rebuke, threaten, curse, or revile the jinn. Thus, it is established in Sahīh Muslim that Abū al-Dardā’ said:

“The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, stood in prayer, and we heard him say, ‘I seek refuge in Allah from you.’ Then he said, ‘I curse you with Allah’s curse,’ three times, and stretched out his hand as though reaching for something. When he had finished praying, we said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, we heard you say something during the prayer that we had never heard you say before, and we saw you stretch out your hand.’

“He said, ‘The enemy of Allah, Iblīs, came with a firebrand to thrust into my face. I said, “I seek refuge in Allah from you,” three times. Then I said three times, “I curse you with Allah’s complete curse,” but he did not retreat. Then I sought to seize him. By Allah, were it not for the supplication of our brother Solomon, he would have been bound in the morning, for the children of the people of Medina to play with.’”

Thus, this hadith mentions seeking refuge from him

---

## Page 9568

and cursing him with Allah’s curse; when he did not retreat at that, the Prophet stretched out his hand toward him.

In the two Sahīhs, Abū Hurayrah reported that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Satan appeared before me and rushed at me to interrupt my prayer. Allah gave me power over him, and I throttled him. I considered tying him to a pillar so that you could see him in the morning, but I remembered the words of my brother Solomon: ‘My Lord, forgive me and grant me a kingdom such as no one after me will have.’ So Allah drove him back, humiliated.”

This hadith agrees with the first and explains it. His words, “I throttled him,” mean “I choked him.” This makes clear that he stretched out his hand to choke him. This was a physical act of repelling his aggression—namely, choking him—and through it his aggression was repelled, so Allah drove him back, humiliated.

The further step of tying him to the pillar, however, would have been an exercise of royal authority, which he refrained from exercising out of regard for Solomon. Our Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, dealt with the jinn just as he dealt with human beings: as a servant-messenger, commanding them to worship and obey Allah. He did not exercise authority over them for a purpose of his own, which would constitute royal authority. He was a servant-messenger, whereas Solomon was a prophet-king. A servant-messenger is superior to a prophet-king, just as the foremost, those brought near to Allah, are superior to the general body of the righteous, the people of the right.

Al-Nasā’ī also narrated, with a chain meeting al-Bukhārī’s criteria, from ‘Ā’ishah that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, was praying when Satan came to him. He seized him, threw him to the ground, and choked him. The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Until I felt the coolness of his tongue against my hand. Were it not for

---

## Page 9569

Solomon’s supplication, he would have been bound by morning for people to see.”

Ahmad and Abū Dāwūd also narrated it from Abū Sa‘īd, with the wording:

“I reached out with my hand and kept choking him until I felt the coolness of his saliva between these two fingers of mine: the thumb and the one next to it.”

He did this during prayer. Scholars have cited this as evidence that such actions are permissible during prayer, like stopping someone from passing in front of the worshipper, killing snakes and scorpions, and praying while engaged in sword fighting.

Scholars have differed over whether a devil from among the jinn invalidates the prayer by passing in front of the worshipper. There are two views, both held within Ahmad’s school, as mentioned by Ibn Hāmid and others.

The first is that it invalidates the prayer, on the basis of this hadith and the Prophet’s statement, when he explained that a black dog passing in front of someone invalidates the prayer:

“The black dog is a devil.”

He thus gave its being a devil as the reason. It is indeed as the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said: the black dog is the devil among dogs, and the jinn frequently take its form, as they also take the form of a black cat. This is because blackness concentrates satanic forces more than other colours do, and has intense heat.

Sacrificial offerings are among the means used to seek favour with the jinn. Some people slaughter animals for the jinn, which is part of the association of partners with Allah that Allah and His Messenger have forbidden. It is also reported that the Prophet forbade sacrifices offered to the jinn.

If the afflicted person recovers through supplication, remembrance of Allah, giving the jinn commands and prohibitions, and rebuking them

---

## Page 9570

and insulting and cursing them, and other such words, the intended aim has been achieved. Even if this causes some of the jinn to fall ill or die, they have wronged themselves, provided that the healer who recites protective prayers and supplicates has not transgressed against them. Many practitioners of incantations do transgress against them: they order the killing of those whom it is unlawful to kill, and may imprison those whom there is no need to imprison. The jinn may therefore fight them over this. Some are killed or made ill by the jinn; in other cases, the jinn do the same to their families, children, or animals.

As for someone who repels their hostility by following the just course commanded by Allah and His Messenger, he has not wronged them. Rather, he is obeying Allah and His Messenger by supporting the oppressed, helping those in distress, and relieving the anguished through lawful means involving neither the association of created beings with Allah nor injustice towards any creature. The jinn do not harm such a person, either because they know he is just or because they are unable to do so.

If, however, the jinn are powerful and he is weak, they may harm him. Such a person should therefore protect himself by reciting passages that seek refuge in Allah, such as the Verse of the Throne and the surahs of refuge, and through prayer, supplication, and other practices that strengthen faith and help him avoid the sins through which they gain power over him. He is striving in Allah’s cause, and this is among the greatest forms of jihad. He should therefore beware lest his sins help the enemy against him. If the matter exceeds his ability, Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity, so he should not expose himself to trials he cannot bear.

Among the greatest means of gaining victory over them is the Verse of the Throne, for it is authentically reported in the Sahīh of

---

## Page 9571

al-Bukhārī, in the hadith narrated by Abū Hurayrah, who said:

“Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, entrusted me with guarding the zakat of Ramadan. Someone came and began scooping up the food. I seized him and said, ‘I will certainly take you before Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.’ He said, ‘I am needy, I have dependants, and my need is desperate.’ So I let him go. The next morning, Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘Abū Hurayrah, what happened to your captive last night?’

“I replied, ‘O Messenger of Allah, he complained of desperate need and dependants, so I took pity on him and let him go.’ He said, ‘He has indeed lied to you, and he will return.’ I knew he would return because Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had said so, and I lay in wait for him. He came and began scooping up the food. I seized him and said, ‘I will certainly take you before Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.’ He said, ‘Let me go! I am needy and have dependants. I will not return.’ I took pity on him and let him go. The next morning, Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to me, ‘Abū Hurayrah, what happened to your captive?’

“I replied, ‘O Messenger of Allah, he complained of need and dependants, so I took pity on him and let him go.’ He said, ‘He has indeed lied to you, and he will return.’ So I lay in wait for him a third time. He came and began scooping up the food. I seized him and said, ‘I will certainly take you before Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. This is the third and last time! You claim you will not return, but then you do.’ He said, ‘Let me go, and I will teach you words through which Allah will benefit you.’ I asked, ‘What are they?’

“He said, ‘When you go to bed, recite the Verse of the Throne: “Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence,” continuing to the end of the verse. A guardian from Allah will remain over you, and no devil will come near you until

---

## Page 9572

morning.’ So I let him go. The next morning, Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asked me, ‘What happened to your captive last night?’

“I replied, ‘O Messenger of Allah, he claimed that he would teach me words through which Allah would benefit me, so I let him go.’ He asked, ‘What are they?’

“I replied, ‘He told me, “When you go to bed, recite the Verse of the Throne from beginning to end: ‘Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence.’” He also told me, “A guardian from Allah will remain over you, and no devil will come near you until morning.”’ They were exceedingly eager for good. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘He has indeed told you the truth, though he is a habitual liar. Do you know whom you have been speaking to for the past three nights, Abū Hurayrah?’ I replied, ‘No.’ He said, ‘That was a devil.’”

Furthermore, countless people have found through experience that its effects in repelling devils and nullifying their workings are beyond measure in both number and power. It has a tremendous effect in driving the devil away from a person, from someone possessed, and from those whom devils assist—such as people given to oppression and anger, those given to lust and revelry, and those who engage in listening sessions involving whistling and clapping. When it is recited over them with sincerity, it drives the devils away, and the illusions the devil conjures are dispelled. The satanic unveilings and powers possessed by the devils’ brethren are likewise nullified, for devils communicate to their allies things that the ignorant suppose to be miraculous gifts granted to Allah’s allies

---

## Page 9573

who are mindful of Him. In reality, these are deceptions practised by devils upon their allies who have incurred divine wrath and gone astray.

An unjust aggressor deserves to be repelled, whether he is a Muslim or an unbeliever. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever is killed defending his property is a martyr; whoever is killed defending his life is a martyr; and whoever is killed defending his religion is a martyr.”

If a wronged person is entitled to defend a wronged person’s property, even by killing the unjust aggressor, how could he not defend his mind, body and honour? For the devil corrupts his mind, torments his body, and may commit with him the same sexual indecency that one human being commits with another. If the devil cannot be repelled except by killing it, then killing it is permissible.

As for abandoning the afflicted person and leaving him without help, this is like abandoning any other victim of injustice. Helping him is a collective obligation when one has the ability. In the two Sahīh collections, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:

“A Muslim is a Muslim’s brother: he neither abandons him nor wrongs him.”

If a person is unable to provide this help, is occupied with a more pressing obligation, or someone else has undertaken it, then it is not obligatory upon him. But if he is able, the duty has become his individual responsibility, and it does not distract him from a more pressing obligation, then he must undertake it.

As for the questioner’s words, “Is this prescribed in Islam?”—this is among the finest deeds and is among the deeds of the prophets and the righteous, for the prophets and the righteous have always

---

## Page 9574

repelled devils from the children of Adam by the means Allah and His Messenger have enjoined, as the Messiah did and as our Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did. Ahmad narrated in his Musnad, and Abū Dāwūd in his Sunan, through Matar ibn ʿAbd al-Rahmān al-Aʿnaq, who said:

“Umm Abān bint al-Wāziʿ ibn Zāriʿ, of ʿĀmir al-ʿAbdī, told me, on her father’s authority, that her grandfather al-Zāriʿ went to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, taking with him a son of his—or a son of his sister—who was suffering from madness.

“She said: ‘My grandfather related: When we came to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, I said, “I have with me a son of mine—or a son of my sister—who is suffering from madness. I have brought him to you so that you may supplicate to Allah for him.”

“‘He said, “Bring him to me.” I went to bring him to him. He was among the mounts, so I untied him, removed his travelling clothes, dressed him in two fine garments, and took him by the hand until I brought him to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He said, “Bring him close to me, with his back towards me.”

“‘[He took hold] of the gathered parts of his garment, at the top and bottom, and began striking his back until I could see the whiteness of his armpits, saying, “Come out, enemy of Allah! Come out, enemy of Allah!” The boy then began looking about as a healthy person would, unlike the way he had looked before. Then the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, seated him before him, called for water for him, wiped his face, and supplicated for him. After the supplication of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, no one in the delegation surpassed him.’”

Ahmad also said in the Musnad:

“ʿAbd Allāh ibn Numayr narrated to us, from ʿUthmān ibn Hakīm, who said: ʿAbd al-Rahmān ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz informed us, from Yaʿlā ibn Murrah, who said: ‘I have indeed seen at the hands of

---

## Page 9575

the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, three things that no one before me has seen and no one after me will see. I once set out on a journey with him. Along the way, we passed a woman sitting with her young son. She said, “O Messenger of Allah, this boy has been afflicted, and his affliction has brought suffering upon us. He is seized by fits I do not know how many times a day.”

‘He said, “Hand him to me.” She lifted him up to him, and he placed him between himself and the pommel of his saddle. Then he opened the boy’s mouth, blew into it three times, and said, “In the name of Allah. I am the servant of Allah. Begone, enemy of Allah!” Then he handed him back to her and said, “Meet us here on our return and tell us how he has fared.”

‘We went on our way and returned to find her in that same place with three sheep. He asked, “How has your boy fared?”

‘She replied, “By the One who sent you with the truth, we have noticed nothing of his affliction up to this very moment. Take these sheep with you.”

‘He said, “Dismount, take one of them, and return the rest.”’” He related the hadith in full.

Wakīʿ narrated to us, saying: Al-Aʿmash narrated to us, from al-Minhāl ibn ʿAmr, from Yaʿlā ibn Murrah, from his father. Wakīʿ said “Murrah,” meaning al-Thaqafī, and did not say “Murrah, from his father”:

“A woman came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, with a young son of hers who was afflicted with madness. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘Come out, enemy of Allah! I am the Messenger of Allah.’

“He said: The boy recovered, and she gave him two rams, some dried curds, and some clarified butter. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘Take the dried curds and clarified butter, take one of the two rams, and return the other to her.’”

---

## Page 9576

ʿAbd al-Razzāq narrated to us: Maʿmar informed us, from ʿAtāʾ ibn al-Sāʾib, from ʿAbd Allah ibn Hafs, from Yaʿlā ibn Murrah al-Thaqafī, who said: “I witnessed three things from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.” He related the hadith, which included the following:

“Then we travelled on and passed a watering place. A woman brought him a son of hers who was possessed by a jinn. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, took hold of his nostril and said, ‘Come out! I am Muhammad, the Messenger of Allah.’

“Then we continued our journey. On our return, we passed that watering place, and the woman brought him some animals for slaughter and some milk. He told her to take the animals back and instructed his Companions to drink the milk, which they did. He asked her about the boy, and she replied, ‘By the One who sent you with the truth, we have seen nothing troubling in him since you left.’”

Even supposing that this had not been transmitted because nothing of the kind had occurred in the presence of the prophets—because the devils had been unable [to] (1) do such things in their presence, although they have done so among us—Allah and His Messenger have commanded us to support the oppressed, relieve those in distress, and benefit Muslims in terms that encompass this.

The two Sahīh collections contain the established report of those who recited al-Fātihah as a healing incantation. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, “How did you know that it is a healing incantation?” He permitted them to accept the agreed payment for healing the person who had been stung through that recitation.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said to the devil who sought to interrupt his prayer, “I seek refuge in Allah from you. I curse you with Allah’s complete curse,” three times.

This is like repelling human aggressors among the disbelievers and the wicked. For although the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his Companions had never seen the Turks and did not shoot with Persian bows or similar weapons needed in battle, nevertheless,

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The text in square brackets is absent from the printed edition.
> Usāma ibn al-Zahrā’ — book coordinator for al-Shāmila.

---

## Page 9577

it is established that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commanded that they be fought and foretold that his community would fight them. It is well known that fighting them effectively requires Persian bows. Were they fought with Arab bows resembling cotton-carding bows, these would be of no avail; rather, they would overpower the Muslims through the strength of their archery. They must therefore be fought with weapons that will overcome them.

Some Muslims said to ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb, “When we see the enemy wearing silk, we feel intimidated.” He replied, “Then you too should wear what they wear.” Furthermore, “the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, instructed his Companions during the compensatory ʿumrah to walk briskly and uncover their right shoulders,” to show the polytheists their strength, even if this had not previously been prescribed. Thus, for the sake of jihad, he did what would not otherwise have been prescribed.

Likewise, curing a possessed person and driving the jinn away from him may require beating. He may be beaten very many times, but the blows fall only upon the jinn, and the possessed person does not feel them. When he regains consciousness, he reports that he felt none of it, and it leaves no effect on his body. He may have been struck on his legs with a stout stick some three hundred or four hundred times, or more or fewer—blows that would have killed the human being had they fallen upon him. Instead, they fall upon the jinn, who cries out, screams, and speaks to those present about various matters. We ourselves have done this and tried it many times, in the presence of large numbers of people; describing these occasions would take a long time.

---

## Page 9578

As for seeking aid against them through spoken or written formulas whose meaning is unknown, this is not prescribed, especially if they involve polytheism, for that is forbidden. Most of what practitioners of incantations say contains polytheism. They may also recite some of the Quran, openly displaying this while concealing the polytheistic words they utter. Seeking healing through what Allah and His Messenger have prescribed suffices, leaving no need for polytheism or its practitioners.

Although Muslims have disagreed over the permissibility of treatment with forbidden substances, such as carrion and pork, they do not disagree that unbelief and polytheism may never be used as treatments, since they are forbidden in every circumstance. This is not comparable to uttering such words under coercion. That is permissible only when the person’s heart remains firm in faith. For such words to take effect, however, the speaker must mean them in his heart; if he utters them while his heart remains firm in faith, they will have no effect.

If Satan knows that his associate treats the incantations with contempt, he will not assist him. Moreover, someone under coercion is compelled to utter such words, whereas there is no necessity to cure an afflicted person by such means, for two reasons:

First, it may be no more effective than the treatment offered by a practitioner of incantations: it may have no effect and may instead make the sufferer worse.

Second, the truth suffices, leaving no need for falsehood.

---

## Page 9579

People fall into three groups in this matter. One group denies that jinn enter human beings. Another repels them through blameworthy incantations. The former deny what exists, while the latter disobey the One who is worshipped—indeed, they disbelieve in Him. The community that follows the middle way acknowledges what is real and true and believes in the one God who is worshipped. Through worshipping Him, supplicating to Him, remembering Him, and using His names and His words, it repels the devils among humans and jinn.

As for questioning jinn, or questioning those who consult them, this is forbidden if it involves believing everything they report and revering the one being questioned. Thus, it is established in Sahīh Muslim and elsewhere that Mu‘āwiya ibn al-Hakam al-Sulamī said:

“I said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, there were things we used to do in the days of ignorance: we used to visit soothsayers.’ He said, ‘Do not visit soothsayers.’”

Also in Sahīh Muslim, through ‘Ubayd Allāh, from Nāfi‘, from Safiyya, from one of the wives of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, it is reported that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Whoever visits a diviner and asks him about something will have no prayer accepted from him for forty days.”

If, however, someone questions such a person to test him and uncover the truth about him, while possessing the means to distinguish his truthfulness from his lies, this is permissible. Thus, it is established in the two Sahīhs:

“The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, questioned Ibn Sayyād, saying, ‘What comes to you?’ He replied, ‘A truthful one and a liar come to me.’ He asked, ‘What do you see?’ He replied, ‘I see a throne upon the water.’ He said, ‘I have hidden something for you.’ He replied, ‘Al-dukh! Al-dukh!’ He said, ‘Away with you! You will never

---

## Page 9580

exceed your station, for you are only one of the soothsayers’ brethren.’”

The same applies when someone listens to what they say and report from the jinn, just as Muslims listen to what unbelievers and evildoers say in order to learn what they have to say and draw lessons from it. Likewise, a wrongdoer’s report may be heard, examined, and verified, without judging it true or false except on evidence. As Allah Most High says:

“If a wrongdoer comes to you with news, verify it.”

It is established in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, on the authority of Abū Hurayra:

“The People of the Book used to read the Torah and explain it in Arabic. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said, ‘When the People of the Book tell you something, neither affirm nor deny it, for they may tell you something true and you would deny it, or something false and you would affirm it. Say, “We believe in what has been revealed to us and what has been revealed to you. Our God and your God is One, and to Him we submit.”’”

Thus, Muslims were permitted to listen to what they said without either affirming or denying it. It has also been reported that Abū Mūsā al-Ash‘arī had received no news of ‘Umar for some time. There was a woman there who had a companion from among the jinn. He asked this jinn about ‘Umar, and the jinn told him that he had left ‘Umar branding the camels collected as charity.

Another report relates that ‘Umar dispatched an army. Someone then came to Medina and reported that they had defeated their enemy, and the news spread. ‘Umar asked about this, and when it was related to him, he said:

“This is Abū al-Haytham, the Muslims’ messenger from among the jinn. The human messenger will arrive later.”

He arrived several days afterward.

---

## Page 9581

Section

It is permissible to write passages from Allah’s Book and words of remembrance of Him in lawful ink for an afflicted person or other sick people, then wash off the writing and give the resulting water to the patient to drink. Ahmad and others explicitly permitted this.

‘Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad said:

I read to my father: Ya‘lā ibn ‘Ubayd narrated to us, from Sufyān, from Muhammad ibn Abī Laylā, from al-Hakam, from Sa‘īd ibn Jubayr, from Ibn ‘Abbās, who said:

“If a woman has difficulty giving birth, the following should be written:

‘In the name of Allah. There is no god but Allah, the Forbearing, the Generous. Glory be to Allah, Lord of the Mighty Throne. All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds.’

‘On the Day they see it, it will be as though they had remained only an evening or its morning.’

‘On the Day they see what they are promised, it will be as though they had remained only an hour of a day. This is a proclamation. Will any be destroyed except the defiantly disobedient people?’”

My father said: “Aswad ibn ‘Āmir narrated a report with the same meaning through his chain of transmission, adding: ‘It is to be written in a clean vessel, and the resulting water given to drink.’”

My father said: “Wakī‘ added to it: ‘She is given it to drink, and the area below her navel is sprinkled with it.’”

‘Abd Allāh said: “I saw my father write for a woman in a bowl or some other clean vessel.”

Abū ‘Amr Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn Hamdān al-Hīrī said:

Al-Hasan ibn Sufyān al-Nasawī informed us; ‘Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad ibn Shabbūya narrated to me; ‘Alī ibn

---

## Page 9582

al-Hasan ibn Shaqīq narrated to us; ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak narrated to us, from Sufyān, from Ibn Abī Laylā, from al-Hakam, from Sa‘īd ibn Jubayr, from Ibn ‘Abbās, who said:

“If a woman has difficulty giving birth, the following should be written:

‘In the name of Allah. There is no god but Allah, the Most High, the Magnificent. There is no god but Allah, the Forbearing, the Generous. Glory be to Allah, and exalted is He, Lord of the Mighty Throne. All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds.’

‘On the Day they see it, it will be as though they had remained only an evening or its morning.’

‘On the Day they see what they are promised, it will be as though they had remained only an hour of a day. This is a proclamation. Will any be destroyed except the defiantly disobedient people?’”

‘Alī said: “It is written on a piece of paper and tied to the woman’s upper arm.”

‘Alī said: “We have tried it and have seen nothing more remarkable. Once she has given birth, she should promptly untie it, then wrap it in a cloth or burn it.”

Here end the words of Shaykh al-Islām Ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah sanctify his soul and illuminate his grave.

---

## Page 9583

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—said:

Section: On the sufficiency of the message and of the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—without the need to follow anyone else in all matters.

Allah established the proof against His creation through His messengers. He, the Exalted, said:

“Indeed, We have revealed to you as We revealed to Noah and the prophets after him,”

up to His words:

“so that mankind will have no argument against Allah after the messengers.”

This verse indicates that, after the messengers, people have no argument under any circumstances, but that they may have had an argument before the messengers. The “first” point refutes those who maintain that people have a universal need for others besides the messengers, such as the imams. The “second” refutes those philosophers and speculative theologians who hold that the proof was established against people before the messengers.

---

## Page 9584

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“O you who believe, obey Allah and obey the Messenger and those in authority among you. If you disagree over anything, refer it to Allah and the Messenger.”

He commanded obedience to those in authority—scholars and rulers—when they do not disagree. This entails that their agreement constitutes binding proof. He commanded them to refer disputes to Allah and the Messenger, thereby ruling out recourse to an imam through unquestioning adherence or to a superior rational argument.

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“Mankind was one community; then Allah sent the prophets as bearers of good tidings and warners, and sent down with them the Scripture in truth to judge between people concerning their differences.”

He thus made clear that it is by the Scripture that He judges between the people of the earth concerning their differences.

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“Whatever you disagree about, its judgment rests with Allah.”

Allah, the Exalted, also said:

“This is a Book sent down to you, so let there be no unease in your heart concerning it, that you may warn by it, and as a reminder to the believers.”

“Follow what has been sent down to you from your Lord, and do not follow other patrons besides Him.”

He thus made it obligatory to follow what He revealed of the Book and wisdom, and prohibited following anyone in its place.

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“Is it not sufficient for them that We have sent down to you the Book, which is recited to them?”

He thus rebuked those who were not content with the revealed Book.

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“O company of jinn and mankind, did there not come to you messengers from among you, relating My verses to you?”

And the following verses.

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“We would never punish until We had sent a messenger.”

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“Those who disbelieved will be driven to Hell in groups.”

And the following verses.

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“Whenever a group is cast into it, its keepers will ask them.”

The two verses. Thus, these indicate

---

## Page 9585

that anyone to whom the Messenger comes and who then opposes him has incurred punishment, even if neither an imam nor a rational argument has reached him; and that no one is punished until the Messenger comes to him, even if an imam or a rational argument has reached him.

Allah, the Exalted, said:

“Whoever obeys Allah and the Messenger will be with those whom Allah has blessed: the prophets, the steadfast affirmers of truth, the martyrs, and the righteous.”

“Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger, He will admit them into Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding there forever. That is the great triumph.”

“And whoever disobeys Allah and His Messenger…”

And the rest of the verse.

He, glorified be He, has mentioned this meaning in several places, making clear that obedience to Allah and His Messenger leads to bliss, while disobedience to Allah leads to misery. This shows that when one obeys Allah and His Messenger, there is no need to obey an imam or follow a rational argument; and when one disobeys Allah and His Messenger, obedience to an imam or adherence to a rational argument is of no benefit.

Evidence for this principle is abundant in the Quran and Sunnah. It is the foundation of Islam: “The testimony that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah, and the testimony that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.” Those endowed with knowledge and faith agree upon it in word and belief, even if some of them contravene it in their conduct and practice. No Muslim scholar doubts that all people are obliged to obey Allah and His Messenger, and that obedience to anyone else is obligatory only where Allah and His Messenger have made it so.

---

## Page 9586

In truth, the fundamental obligation is obedience to Allah alone. Yet there is no way to know all that He commands and communicates except through the messengers. Whoever conveys on His behalf either conveys His commands and His words—in which case he must be obeyed in everything he commands and believed in everything he reports—or is someone else, who is to be obeyed in some circumstances but not others. Such are rulers, who must be obeyed within the sphere of their authority so long as they do not command disobedience to Allah; and scholars, whom those seeking a fatwa or receiving an instruction must obey concerning what they declare obligatory for them, whether they are conveying Allah’s commands or exercising independent legal judgment in matters in which their followers are obliged to obey them. This includes religious teachers and temporal leaders wherever obedience to them has been commanded: following prayer leaders in prayer, pilgrimage leaders during the pilgrimage, military commanders on campaign, judges in their rulings, and rightly guided religious teachers in their guidance, and the like.

The point of this principle is that anyone who holds obedience to an appointed imam to be unconditionally obligatory, whether in belief or in practice, has gone astray in this respect. Such are the misguided leaders of the Imāmī Rāfidah, who posit an infallible imam in every age whom people are obliged to obey. Yet no one after the Messenger is infallible, nor must anyone after him be obeyed in everything. Among the members of the Prophet’s household whom they have designated, one was a rightly guided caliph who had to be obeyed just as the caliphs before him had to be obeyed: namely, ‘Alī. Others were leaders in knowledge and religion, entitled to the same rights as their peers among the leaders of knowledge and religion, such as ‘Alī ibn al-Husayn, Abū Ja‘far al-Bāqir, and Ja‘far ibn Muhammad al-Sādiq. Others were below that rank.

---

## Page 9587

The same applies to anyone who calls for following a religious shaykh in every way, without qualification or exception, singling him out from his peers—such as Shaykh ‘Adī, Shaykh Ahmad, Shaykh ‘Abd al-Qādir, Shaykh Haywah, and others like them. It likewise applies to anyone who calls for unconditionally following a leading scholar, such as one of the four imams, in everything he says, commands, and forbids. The same applies to anyone who commands obedience to kings, commanders, judges, and governors in everything they command and forbid, without qualification or exception.

These people, however, do not claim infallibility for their leaders, except for extremists among the followers of shaykhs such as Shaykh ‘Adī, Sa‘d al-Madīnī ibn Hamawayh, and others like them. They make claims about them resembling those made by extremists concerning the imams of Banū Hāshim: first infallibility, then superiority to prophethood, and finally divinity.

As for many followers of leading scholars and religious shaykhs, their conduct and inclinations resemble those of someone who considers following his leader obligatory, yet neither expresses this in words nor consciously holds it as a belief. His conduct thus contradicts his beliefs, like sinners driven by their desires. Such people are in a better state than those who consider this obligatory and hold it as a conviction.

Likewise, the followers of kings and leaders are as Allah describes them when He says:

“Indeed, we obeyed our masters and our great men, and they led us astray from the way.”

They obey in their conduct and actions, submitting to their leaders. Most do so without any religious conviction, while among them are

---

## Page 9588

those who combine this with a religious conviction. Yet obedience to the Messenger is possible only with knowledge of what he brought and the ability to act upon it. When knowledge and ability diminish, the time becomes a period of interruption in that particular matter, while in other matters it is a time of religious calling and prophethood. Reflect on this principle, for it is immensely beneficial. And Allah knows best.

Likewise, anyone among the adherents of philosophy, speculative theology, and Sufism who sets up analogical reasoning, reason, or spiritual experience as an unconditional authority—or anyone among the adherents of speculative theology, legal opinion, philosophy, and Sufism who gives it precedence over the Messenger—is like someone who sets up a person as such an authority. Unconditional adherence is therefore inseparably tied to the Messenger and has no place apart from him.

Section

The first religious innovation to appear in Islam, and the one most explicitly condemned in the Sunnah and transmitted reports, was the innovation of the renegade Harūriyyah. The first of them said to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to his face:

“Be just, O Muhammad, for you have not acted justly!”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commanded that they be killed and fought. The Companions of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, fought them alongside the Commander of the Faithful, ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib. There are numerous hadiths from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, describing them and condemning them

---

## Page 9589

and commanding that they be fought. Ahmad ibn Hanbal said:

“The hadith concerning the Khawārij has been authentically transmitted through ten routes.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“One of you would consider his prayer insignificant compared with theirs, his fasting compared with theirs, and his recitation compared with theirs. They recite the Quran, but it does not pass beyond their throats. They pass out of Islam as an arrow passes through its quarry. Wherever you encounter them, kill them, for whoever kills them will have a reward with Allah on the Day of Resurrection.”

They have two well-known characteristics by which they have parted company with the Muslim community and its leaders:

The first is their departure from the Sunnah and their declaring something evil when it is not, or good when it is not. This is what they displayed to the Prophet’s face, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, when Dhū al-Khuwaysirah al-Tamīmī said to him:

“Be just, for you have not acted justly!”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, replied:

“Woe to you! Who would act justly if I did not? You would indeed be disappointed and at a loss if I did not act justly!”

By saying, “For you have not acted justly,” he characterized the Prophet’s action, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as foolish and unjust. His demand, “Be just,” was a command to adopt a manner of distribution that he himself considered good, although it was improper.

This characteristic is shared by innovations that oppose the Sunnah. Their proponents necessarily affirm what the Sunnah denies and deny what the Sunnah affirms, and deem good what the Sunnah deems evil, or evil what the Sunnah deems good. Otherwise, it would not be an innovation. Scholars may sometimes fall into this through error in particular matters; the people of innovation, however, oppose the clear and well-known Sunnah.

---

## Page 9590

The Khawārij held it possible that the Messenger himself might act unjustly and lead people astray through his Sunnah. They did not regard obeying and following him as obligatory. They believed him only in what he conveyed of the Quran, not in the Sunnah he prescribed which, they claimed, contradicted the Quran’s apparent meaning. Most proponents of innovation other than the Khawārij follow them in this regard in reality.

They hold that if the Messenger were to say something contrary to their doctrine, they would not follow him, as is related of ʿAmr ibn ʿUbayd concerning the hadith of “the truthful and trustworthy one.” They merely seek to evade the evidence against them, either by rejecting the transmitted report or by reinterpreting its content. Thus, they sometimes impugn the chain of transmission and sometimes the text. In reality, they neither follow nor take as their guide the Sunnah that the Messenger actually brought—or even the Quran as it truly is.

The second distinguishing characteristic of the Khawārij and the people of innovation is that they declare people disbelievers on account of sins and misdeeds. As a consequence, they deem the blood and property of Muslims lawful, regard the abode of Islam as an abode of war, and consider their own abode to be the abode of faith. This is also the position of the majority of the Rāfidah, the majority of the Muʿtazilah, the Jahmiyyah, and a group of extremists affiliated with the scholars of hadith and jurisprudence and their theologians.

This, then, is the root of the innovations identified as such by the explicit Sunnah of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and by the consensus of the early generations: treating what is excused as a sin, and treating a sin as disbelief.

---

## Page 9591

A Muslim should therefore beware of these two pernicious principles and what they engender among some Muslims: disparaging and cursing other Muslims, and deeming their blood and property lawful. These two principles run counter to the Sunnah and the Muslim community. Whoever opposes what the Sunnah has brought or prescribed is an innovator who has departed from the Sunnah. Whoever declares Muslims disbelievers because of something he considers sinful—whether it is actually part of the religion or not—and treats them as disbelievers has separated himself from the Muslim community. Most innovations and deviant inclinations arise from these two principles.

The first involves specious reasoning based on faulty interpretation or faulty analogy. A person may receive an inauthentic hadith attributed to the Messenger, or a report from someone other than the Messenger whom he follows in that matter, although that person was mistaken. Alternatively, he may incorrectly interpret a verse from Allah’s Book, a hadith attributed to Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—whether authentic or weak—or a transmitted report, whether accepted or rejected. Or he may employ a faulty analogy, or form an opinion that he believes to be correct when it is mistaken.

Analogical reasoning, personal opinion, and spiritual intuition account for most of the errors of speculative theologians, Sufis, and a group of those engaged in jurisprudence. Faulty interpretation of authentic or weak texts accounts for most of the errors of various groups of speculative theologians, hadith scholars, uncritical followers, Sufis, and those engaged in jurisprudence.

---

## Page 9592

As for declaring someone a disbeliever on account of a sin or a belief consistent with the Sunnah, this is the doctrine of the Kharijites. Declaring someone a disbeliever on account of a belief consistent with the Sunnah is the doctrine of the Rāfidah, the Muʿtazilah, and many others. As for declaring someone a disbeliever on account of an innovated belief, I have explained this elsewhere.

Even short of declaring someone a disbeliever, some of these interpretations may lead to impermissible hatred, censure, and punishment—which constitute transgression—or to withholding love, supplication, and kindness—which constitutes neglect. All of this amounts to violating the rights of Allah Most High or those of His creatures, as I have explained elsewhere.

For this reason, Ahmad ibn Hanbal said to one of his companions:
“Most of people’s errors arise from interpretation and analogical reasoning.”

---

## Page 9593

Shaykh al-Islām, leader of the imams and the Muslims, Abū al-‘Abbās Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Halīm ibn ‘Abd al-Salām Ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah sanctify his soul—said:

All praise is due to Allah. We seek His help and forgiveness, and we seek refuge in Allah from the evil within ourselves and from our evil deeds. Whoever Allah guides, none can lead astray; and whoever He leads astray, none can guide. I bear witness that none is worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and Messenger. He sent him with guidance and the religion of truth to make it prevail over all religion, and sufficient is Allah as a witness. May Allah bestow abundant blessings and peace upon him, his family, and his Companions.

“A Comprehensive Principle”

On holding fast to the Book of Allah and the obligation to follow it; explaining how it provides guidance in everything people need concerning their religion; that salvation and happiness lie in following it, and misery in opposing it; and its teaching that the Sunnah and the Muslim community must be followed.

Allah Most High said:

“He said, ‘Descend from it, both of you together, as enemies to one another. Then, if guidance comes to you from Me, whoever follows My guidance will neither go astray nor suffer.’”

“But whoever turns away from My remembrance will have a life of hardship, and We will raise him up blind on the Day of Resurrection.”

“He will say,

---

## Page 9594

‘My Lord, why have You raised me up blind when I used to see?’”

“He will say, ‘Thus Our signs came to you, but you forgot them; and thus today you will be forgotten.’”

Ibn ‘Abbās said: “Allah has guaranteed that whoever recites the Quran and acts upon its teachings will neither go astray in this world nor suffer in the Hereafter.” Then he recited this verse.

In the other surah:

“Whoever follows My guidance—no fear will be upon them, nor will they grieve.”

“But those who disbelieve and deny Our signs—they are the inhabitants of the Fire, remaining therein forever.”

Allah Most High also said:

“Alif Lām Mīm Sād.”

“A Book has been sent down to you—so let there be no distress in your heart on its account—that you may warn through it, and as a reminder to the believers.”

“Follow what has been sent down to you from your Lord, and do not follow any protectors besides Him. How little you take heed!”

Allah Most High also said:

“And this is a blessed Book which We have sent down, so follow it and fear Allah, that you may receive mercy.”

“Lest you say, ‘The Scripture was sent down only to two communities before us, and we were unaware of what they studied.’”

“Or lest you say, ‘Had the Scripture been sent down to us, we would have been better guided than they.’ Now clear evidence has come to you from your Lord, and guidance and mercy. Who, then, is more unjust than one who denies Allah’s signs and turns away from them? We will repay those who turn away from Our signs with a terrible punishment for their turning away.”

Allah Most High also said:

“O children of Adam, if messengers from among you come to you, relating My signs to you, then whoever fears Allah and mends their ways—no fear will be upon them, nor will they grieve.”

“But those who deny Our signs and arrogantly reject them—they are the inhabitants of the Fire, remaining therein forever.”

---

## Page 9595

Allah Most High also said:

“Whenever a group is thrown into it, its keepers will ask them, ‘Did no warner come to you?’”

“They will say, ‘Yes, a warner did come to us, but we denied him and said, “Allah has sent down nothing. You are only in great error.”’”

Allah Most High also said:

“Those who disbelieved will be driven to Hell in groups until, when they reach it, its gates will be opened and its keepers will say to them, ‘Did messengers from among yourselves not come to you, reciting your Lord’s signs to you and warning you of the meeting of this Day of yours?’ They will say, ‘Yes, but the decree of punishment has come into effect against the disbelievers.’”

Allah Most High also said:

“None dispute Allah’s signs except those who disbelieve, so do not be deceived by their movement throughout the lands.”

Further on, He says:

“Those who dispute Allah’s signs without any authority having come to them—such disputing is greatly hateful to Allah and to those who believe. Thus does Allah seal every heart of an arrogant tyrant.”

Further on, He says:

“We will surely support Our messengers and those who believe, both in the life of this world and on the Day when the witnesses stand forth.”

Further on, He says:

“We certainly gave Moses guidance and caused the Children of Israel to inherit the Scripture,”

“as guidance and a reminder for people of understanding.”

“So be patient; indeed, Allah’s promise is true. Ask forgiveness for your sin, and glorify your Lord with praise in the evening and the morning.”

“Indeed, those who dispute Allah’s signs without any authority having come to them have nothing in their hearts but a desire for greatness that they will never attain. So seek refuge in Allah. He alone is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing.”

His words, “dispute Allah’s signs without any authority having come to them,” make clear that it is not permissible to counter the Book of Allah with anything other than the Book of Allah—not with anyone’s actions or commands, nor with state authority or political policy. For that is the condition of those who dispute Allah’s signs without

---

## Page 9596

any authority having come to them.

However, Allah’s verses may include both abrogating and abrogated verses, in which case an abrogated verse may be countered with one that abrogates it, as Allah Most High said:

“Whatever verse We abrogate or cause to be forgotten, We bring one better than it or like it.”

Allah Most High also said:

“The foolish among the people will say, ‘What has turned them away from the direction of prayer they used to face?’ Say, ‘To Allah belong the east and the west. He guides whom He wills to a straight path.’”

There are numerous similar examples.

Allah Most High said:

“Mankind was one community. Then Allah sent the prophets as bearers of good tidings and warners, and sent down with them the Scripture in truth to judge between people concerning their differences. None differed over it except those who had been given it, after clear proofs had come to them, out of rivalry among themselves. Then Allah, by His permission, guided those who believed to the truth concerning which they had differed. Allah guides whom He wills to a straight path.”

Allah Most High also said:

“Alif Lām Rā.”

“A Book We have sent down to you so that you may bring mankind out of darkness into light, by their Lord’s permission, to the path of the Almighty, the Praiseworthy.”

Allah Most High also said:

“It is He who sends down clear signs to His servant to bring you out of darkness into light.”

Allah Most High also said:

“There has come to you from Allah a light and a clear Book.”

“Through it Allah guides those who seek His pleasure to the ways of peace, brings them out of darkness into light by His permission, and guides them to a straight path.”

Allah Most High also said:

“O you who believe, if you obey a group of those who were given the Scripture, they will turn you back into disbelievers after you have believed.”

“And how could you disbelieve when Allah’s verses are being recited to you and His Messenger is among you? Whoever holds fast to Allah has indeed been guided to a path

---

## Page 9597

that is straight.”

“O you who believe, fear Allah as He should be feared, and do not die except as Muslims.”

“And hold firmly, all together, to the rope of Allah.”

Thus, we are commanded to hold fast to Allah’s rope, which is His Book, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“This Quran is a rope stretched out, one end in Allah’s Hand and the other in your hands. Hold fast to it, for you will never go astray as long as you hold fast to it.”

In another hadith:

“It is Allah’s firm rope.”

Then Allah Most High said:

“And do not become divided. Remember Allah’s favor upon you: you were enemies, and He brought your hearts together, so that by His favor you became brothers.”

And so the verses continue.

Allah Most High also said:

“On the Day when We raise up in every community a witness against them from among themselves, We will bring you as a witness against these people. We have sent down to you the Book as a clarification of all things, a guidance, a mercy, and good tidings for Muslims.”

Allah Most High also said:

“It is not a fabricated account, but a confirmation of what came before it, a detailed explanation of all things, and a guidance and mercy for people who believe.”

Allah Most High also said:

“Alif Lām Mīm.”

“We send down of the Quran that which is a healing and mercy for the believers

## Page 9601

with those whom Allah has blessed: the prophets, the truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous. What excellent companions they are!”

Allah Most High also said:
