These texts make it obligatory to follow the Messenger even if we do not find what he said explicitly stated in the Book, just as those verses make it obligatory to follow the Book even if we do not find what the Book says explicitly stated in a hadith from the Messenger apart from the Book. We must therefore follow the Book, and we must follow the Messenger. Following either is following the other, for the Messenger conveyed the Book, and the Book commands obedience to the Messenger.

The Book and the Messenger never contradict one another, just as no part of the Book contradicts another. Allah Most High said:

“Had it been from anyone other than Allah, they would have found much contradiction in it.”

---

## Page 9602

There are many hadiths from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning the obligation to follow the Book and the obligation to follow his Sunnah, such as his saying:

“Let me not find any of you reclining on his couch when a directive of mine reaches him concerning something I have commanded or forbidden, saying, ‘Let this Quran decide between us and you: whatever we find lawful in it, we deem lawful, and whatever we find forbidden in it, we deem forbidden.’ Indeed, I have been given the Book and something like it along with it. Indeed, it is like the Quran, or greater.”

This hadith is transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, through several routes in the Sunan and Musnad collections, including the reports of Abū Thaʿlabah, Abū Rāfiʿ, Abū Hurayrah, and others.

In Sahīh Muslim, Jābir reports that he said in his Farewell Sermon:

“I have left among you that which, if you hold fast to it, you will never go astray thereafter: the Book of Allah Most High.”

It is also reported in the Sahīh that ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Awfā was asked, “Did the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, leave a will?”

He replied, “No.”

He was asked, “Then how was making a will prescribed for people?”

He replied, “He enjoined adherence to the Book of Allah.”

The Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, explains the Quran. Thus, it explains the number of prayers, the amount to be recited in them, and audible and quiet recitation. It likewise explains the prescribed amounts of zakat and its minimum thresholds, as well as the pilgrimage rites and the prescribed numbers for circumambulating the House, going between Safā and Marwah, casting pebbles at the pillars, and similar matters. When this Sunnah is established as authentic, all Muslims agree on the obligation of

---

## Page 9603

following it. Some of his Sunnah may be thought to conflict with the apparent meaning of the Quran or to add to it, such as the Sunnah specifying the minimum value of stolen property subject to punishment and requiring the stoning of the muhsan adulterer—one possessing the requisite marital status. The Companions, those who followed them in righteousness, and all other groups of Muslims likewise hold that this Sunnah must be followed, except those who disputed this among the renegade Khawārij, concerning whom the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Any one of you would consider his prayer insignificant compared with theirs, his fasting compared with theirs, and his recitation compared with theirs. They recite the Quran, but it does not pass beyond their throats. They pass out of Islam as an arrow passes through its quarry. Wherever you encounter them, kill them, for whoever fights them will have a reward with Allah on the Day of Resurrection for killing them.”

Numerous authentic hadiths have been transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, describing and condemning them and commanding that they be fought.

Ahmad ibn Hanbal said, “The hadith concerning the Khawārij has been authentically transmitted through ten routes.” Muslim transmitted the hadith concerning them in his Sahīh through ten routes, seemingly the very ones to which Ahmad ibn Hanbal referred, since Muslim learned from Ahmad. Al-Bukhārī also transmitted the hadith concerning them through several routes.

The first of these people said to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, “Muhammad, be just, for you have not been just!” One who considers it possible for him to wrong him and thus fail to act justly is like one who holds obedience to him obligatory in the very matter in which he has done wrong. Yet they hold that what he conveyed from Allah must be followed. This stems from their ignorance and inconsistency. Hence the Prophet said

---

## Page 9604

(may Allah bless him and grant him peace):

“Woe to you! Who would be just if I were not just?” He also said, “You would indeed have failed and lost if I were not just.”

That is, if you follow someone who is unjust, you are a failure and have suffered loss.

He also said:

“Does He who is in heaven trust me, while you do not trust me?”

He means: If Allah has entrusted me with conveying His words, will you not trust me to discharge the trust owed to Allah? Allah Most High says:

“It is not for a prophet to betray a trust.”

In short, the Quran requires obedience to him in his judgments and his distribution of wealth, and condemns anyone who turns away from him in either matter. Thus, concerning his judgments, Allah Most High says:

“But no, by your Lord, they will not believe until they make you judge concerning what they dispute among themselves, then find within themselves no discomfort over what you have decided and submit completely.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Have you not seen those who claim to believe in what has been revealed to you and what was revealed before you? They wish to seek judgment from false authorities, although they were commanded to reject them. Satan wishes to lead them far astray.”

“When they are told, ‘Come to what Allah has revealed and to the Messenger,’ you see the hypocrites turning away from you in aversion.”

“How will it be when disaster strikes them because of what their own hands have done, and then they come to you, swearing by Allah, ‘We intended nothing but goodwill and reconciliation’?”

“Those are the ones whose hearts Allah knows. So turn away from them, admonish them, and speak to them words that reach their very souls.”

“We sent no messenger except to be obeyed by Allah’s permission. If, when they wronged themselves, they had come to you and asked Allah’s forgiveness, and the Messenger had asked forgiveness for them

---

## Page 9605

they would have found Allah ever accepting of repentance, Most Merciful.” Allah Most High also says:

“They say, ‘We believe in Allah and in the Messenger, and we obey.’ Then a group of them turns away after that. Those are not believers.”

“When they are called to Allah and His Messenger so that he may judge between them, a group of them turns away.”

“But if the right is theirs, they come to him in submission.”

“Is there a disease in their hearts? Or have they doubted? Or do they fear that Allah and His Messenger will deal unjustly with them? Rather, it is they who are the wrongdoers.”

“The only response of the believers, when they are called to Allah and His Messenger so that he may judge between them, is to say, ‘We hear and we obey.’ Those are the successful.”

“Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger, fears Allah and is mindful of Him—it is they who will triumph.”

Regarding his distribution of charitable funds and fay’ revenues, Allah says concerning charitable funds:

“Among them are those who criticize you concerning charitable funds. If they are given some, they are pleased; but if they are not given any, they become resentful.”

“If only they had been content with what Allah and His Messenger gave them and had said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us. Allah will give us of His bounty, and so will His Messenger. To Allah alone we turn in hope.’”

Concerning fay’ revenues, He says:

“Whatever Allah has bestowed upon His Messenger from the people of the towns belongs to Allah, the Messenger, close relatives, orphans, the needy and the stranded traveller, so that it does not circulate only among the rich among you. Whatever the Messenger gives you, take it; and whatever he forbids you, refrain from it. And fear Allah; indeed, Allah is severe in punishment.”

“For the poor emigrants who were driven from their homes”—the three verses.

Thus, anyone who impugns any of his judgments or distributions—like the Khawārij—casts aspersions on

---

## Page 9606

the Book of Allah, opposes the Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and parts company with the Muslim community. The devil of the Khawārij was held in check while the Muslims were united during the reigns of the three caliphs, Abū Bakr, Umar and Uthmān. When the community became divided during the caliphate of Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, the devil of the Khawārij found an opportunity for rebellion. They rebelled and declared Alī, Mu'āwiyah and their supporters disbelievers. They were fought by Alī ibn Abī Tālib, whose party was the closer of the two to the truth, as is authentically reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“A renegade faction will emerge at a time of division among the people. The one of the two parties closer to the truth will kill them.”

Thus, when people such as Ibn Abbās, Umar ibn Abd al-Azīz and others debated them, they demonstrated the falsity of their position through the Book and the Balance. Ibn Abbās did so when they objected to Alī ibn Abī Tālib fighting his opponents at the Battle of the Camel while forbidding the pursuit of those who fled, the killing of their wounded, and the taking of their property and children as spoils. The Khawārij argued that the Book of Allah recognizes only believers and disbelievers: if those people were believers, fighting them was unlawful; and if they were disbelievers, their blood, property and children were lawful spoils.

Ibn Abbās answered them by pointing out that the Quran establishes that Ā'ishah is the Mother of the Believers. He explained that sexual relations with the Mothers of the Believers are forbidden: whoever denies that she is their mother contradicts the Book of Allah, and whoever considers sexual relations with his mother lawful contradicts the Book of Allah.

Their error lay in supposing that fighting a believer could never be lawful under any circumstances. This is also what led astray those of the Shia who went astray, for they supposed that anyone who fought Alī was a disbeliever;

---

## Page 9607

yet this contradicts the Quran. The Most High says:

“If two groups of believers fight, make peace between them. But if one of them transgresses against the other, fight the transgressing group until it returns to Allah’s command. If it returns, make peace between them with justice and act equitably. Indeed, Allah loves those who act equitably.”

“The believers are but brothers, so make peace between your two brothers.”

He, glorified be He, thus describes them as believers despite their fighting, and commands that, if one group transgresses against the other, the transgressing group should be fought. He did not command fighting either group at the outset. He then commands that, when one of them returns to His command, peace should be made between them with justice. He says:

“The believers are but brothers, so make peace between your two brothers.”

The Quran thus affirms their faith and brotherhood despite the fighting and transgression, and commands fighting the transgressing group where Allah has commanded this.

Likewise, when Umar ibn Abd al-Azīz debated them and they acknowledged the obligation to refer to what the Companions had transmitted from the Messenger concerning the obligatory requirements of prayer, Umar explained that they must likewise refer to what the Companions had transmitted from him, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning the obligation of stoning and the minimum threshold for zakat. Drawing a distinction between these matters would be to distinguish between equivalent cases. They therefore accepted this.

Similarly, when they rejected the appointment of men as arbitrators, Ibn Abbās argued against them by pointing out that Allah had said, concerning a married couple, that if a breach between them is feared, an arbitrator should be appointed from his family and another from hers. He said:

“If they both desire reconciliation, Allah will bring about harmony between them.”

He also commanded that compensation for killed game be assessed in equivalent livestock, as determined by two just men among you. Thus, whoever rejects arbitration altogether has contradicted the Book of Allah Most High. Ibn Abbās also stated that

---

## Page 9608

arbitration between two rulers to spare the Muslim community’s blood is more warranted than arbitration between spouses or over the killing of game.

Here Ibn Abbās reasons by comparison and a fortiori analogy, which belongs to the Balance. Thus, the argument against them was established through the Book and the Balance. Allah Most High says:

“O you who believe, obey Allah and obey the Messenger and those in authority among you. If you disagree over anything, refer it to Allah and the Messenger, if you believe in Allah and the Last Day. That is best and leads to the best outcome.”

He, glorified be He, commands obedience to Himself, His Messenger, and those in authority among us. He also commands us, if we disagree over anything, to refer it to Allah and the Messenger. This shows that whatever the believers disagree over, they must refer it to Allah and the Messenger. A command made conditional does not apply when its condition is absent. This therefore indicates that, when they do not disagree, this command does not apply. That can only be because, when they do not disagree, they are following guidance and obeying Allah and His Messenger. There is then no need for a command to perform the obedience to Allah and the Messenger that they are already practising.

This also indicates that, when they do not disagree but reach agreement, they do not agree upon misguidance. Were it possible for them to agree upon misguidance, referring matters to Allah and the Messenger would then be even more necessary than when they disagree, since one of the two groups may be obeying Allah and the Messenger. If, in this situation, they are commanded to refer matters to Allah and the Messenger so that a group among them—having departed from that obedience—may return to it, then all the more should they be commanded to do so

---

## Page 9609

if all of them were presumed to have departed from that obedience, with even greater reason and necessity. Moreover, He said to them:

“And hold firmly to the rope of Allah all together and do not become divided. And remember Allah’s favour upon you: when you were enemies, He united your hearts, and by His favour you became brothers. You were on the brink of a pit of Fire, and He saved you from it.”

Since He forbade them from becoming divided without qualification, this indicates that they cannot agree upon falsehood. For if they agreed upon falsehood, following the truth would be obligatory, even though this would entail their division. He also explained that He united their hearts, so that by His favour they became brothers, as He said:

“It is He who supported you with His help and with the believers”

“and united their hearts. Had you spent all that is on earth, you could not have united their hearts, but Allah united them.”

Thus, when their hearts are united rather than divided over a matter, this is part of the fullness of Allah’s favour upon them and among the blessings He has bestowed on them. It therefore cannot be agreement upon falsehood, for Allah Most High knows all matters best.

End. All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds.

---

## Page 9610

Shaykh al-Islām Taqī al-Dīn Abū al-‘Abbās Ahmad ibn ‘Abd al-Halīm ibn ‘Abd al-Salām ibn Abī al-Qāsim ibn Muhammad ibn Taymiyyah al-Harrānī—may Allah be pleased with him and illuminate his grave—said:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds.

A Beneficial Principle

On the obligation to hold fast to the message, explaining that happiness and guidance lie in following the Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—while misguidance and misery lie in opposing him; that all good that exists, whether general or particular, comes by way of the Messenger; that every evil in the world affecting an individual servant is caused by opposing the Messenger or being ignorant of what he brought; and that the happiness of Allah’s servants, in this life and the Hereafter, lies in following the message.

The message is indispensable to Allah’s servants: they cannot do without it, and their need for it exceeds their need for anything else. The message is the spirit, light and life of the world. How can the world be set right if it is deprived of spirit, life and light? The world is dark and accursed except where the sun of the message has risen. Likewise, unless the sun of the message shines in a servant’s heart and he receives something of its life and spirit, he remains in darkness and is among the dead. Allah says:

---

## Page 9611

—exalted is He:

“Is one who was dead, whom We brought to life and gave a light by which to walk among people, like one who is in darkness, never to emerge from it?”

This describes the believer: he was dead in the darkness of ignorance, then Allah brought him to life through the spirit of the message and the light of faith, and gave him a light by which to walk among people. The disbeliever, however, is dead at heart, dwelling in darkness.

Allah Most High called His message a spirit; when the spirit is absent, life is lost. Allah Most High says:

“And thus We have revealed to you a spirit by Our command. You did not know what the Book was, nor faith, but We made it a light by which We guide whom We will of Our servants.”

Here He mentions the two foundations: spirit and light. The spirit is life, and the light is light. Likewise, Allah illustrates the revelation He sent down as life and light for hearts through the examples of water, which He sends down from the sky to give life to the earth, and fire, which produces light. This is expressed in His words, exalted is He:

“He sends down water from the sky, and valleys flow according to their capacity, while the torrent carries swelling foam. A similar foam comes from what they heat in the fire to make ornaments or useful objects. Thus Allah illustrates truth and falsehood. As for the foam, it is cast away, while what benefits people remains in the earth. Thus Allah sets forth examples.”

He thus likens knowledge to water sent down from the sky, for it gives life to hearts, just as

---

## Page 9612

water gives life to bodies. He likens hearts to valleys because they hold knowledge just as valleys hold water: one heart holds much knowledge, as one valley holds much water, while another heart holds little knowledge, as another valley holds little water. The Most High tells us that foam rises upon the torrent as the water churns, then is cast away—that is, discarded and lost from sight—while what benefits people remains and settles in the earth. Likewise, desires and doubts mingle within hearts. As truth grows within them, those desires and doubts are stirred up, then cast away, while faith and the Quran remain firmly established, benefiting both the person whose heart contains them and other people. He says:

“A similar foam comes from what they heat in the fire to make ornaments or useful objects. Thus Allah illustrates truth and falsehood.”

This is the other illustration, that of fire: the first concerns life, and the second illumination.

These two illustrations have their counterparts in the two mentioned in Surah al-Baqarah, in the words of the Most High, from:

“Their likeness is that of one who kindled a fire,”

to His words:

“Or like a rainstorm from the sky,”

and on to the end of the verse.

As for the disbeliever, he is lifeless in the darkness of disbelief and associating partners with Allah. Although he possesses an animal life, he lacks the higher spiritual life brought about by faith, through which a servant attains happiness and success in this world and the Hereafter. For Allah, glorified is He, made the messengers intermediaries between Himself and His servants to teach them what benefits and harms them, and to perfect all that serves their well-being in their worldly lives and their final return. They were all sent to call people to Allah, to make known the path leading to Him, and to explain what their condition will be after reaching Him.

---

## Page 9613

The first principle encompasses affirming Allah’s attributes, His oneness, and divine decree, and recounting Allah’s dealings with His allies and enemies—the stories He has related to His servants and the parables He has set forth for them. The second principle encompasses the detailed religious laws, commands, prohibitions, and permissions, and an explanation of what Allah loves and what He dislikes. The third principle encompasses belief in the Last Day, Paradise and Hell, and reward and punishment.

Creation and divine command revolve around these three principles, and happiness and success depend upon them. There is no way to know them except through the messengers, for reason cannot discover their details or comprehend their realities, although it may recognize in general terms why they are indispensable. It is like a sick person who recognizes his need for medicine and someone to treat him, but cannot discern the precise nature of his illness or how to apply the appropriate remedy.

A servant’s need for the divine message is far greater than a sick person’s need for medicine. The worst conceivable consequence of having no physician is the death of the body. But if a servant does not receive the light and life brought by the divine message, his heart dies a death from which there is no hope of ever returning to life, or he becomes wretched

---

## Page 9614

without ever knowing happiness. There is no success except through following the Messenger, for Allah has granted success exclusively to his believing followers and supporters, as He, Most High, says:

“So those who believe in him, honour him, support him, and follow the light sent down with him—it is they who will succeed.”

That is, none but they will succeed. As He, Most High, says:

“Let there be among you a community calling to goodness, enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong. It is they who will succeed.”

He has singled these people out for success, just as He has singled out for guidance and success those who fear Him: those who believe in the unseen, establish prayer, spend from what He has provided them, believe in what was revealed to His Messenger and what was revealed before him, and are certain of the Hereafter. Thus it is clear that guidance and success depend entirely upon the divine message: without it, neither exists.

This is something upon which all the divinely revealed Books agree and with which all the messengers were sent. That is why Allah has related to us the accounts of the nations that rejected the messengers and the fate that befell them. He preserved their remains and dwellings as a lesson and an admonition for those who came after them.

Likewise, He transformed some into apes and swine because they opposed their prophets. The same applies to those whom He caused the earth to swallow, those upon whom He sent stones from heaven, those whom He drowned in the sea, those against whom He sent the mighty blast, and those whom He seized with various punishments. All this was only because they opposed the messengers, turned away from what they brought, and took protectors besides Him. This is His way—glory be to Him—with those who oppose His messengers and turn away from what they have brought

---

## Page 9615

and follow a path other than theirs. For this reason, Allah—glory be to Him—has preserved the remains of those who denied the messengers, so that we may learn from them and heed the warning, lest we act as they did and suffer what befell them. As He, Most High, says:

“Indeed, We will bring down upon the people of this town a punishment from heaven because of their defiant disobedience. And We have certainly left a clear sign of it for people who use reason.”

He, Most High, also says:

“Then We destroyed the others. Indeed, you pass by their ruins in the morning and at night. Will you not then use reason?”

That is, you pass by them during the day, in the morning, and at night. Then He says: “Will you not then use reason?”

Concerning the towns of the people of Lot, He, Most High, says:

“And We rained down upon them stones of baked clay. Surely in that are signs for those who discern. And those towns lie along an established route.”

This means that their towns lie along an established road, where those passing by can see them.

He, Most High, also says:

“Have they not travelled through the land and seen what was the fate of those before them?”

Such passages occur frequently in the Noble Book: Allah—glory be to Him—tells of the destruction of those who opposed the messengers and the deliverance of their followers.

Thus, in Surah al-Shuarā, He—glory be to Him—relates the accounts of Moses, Abraham, Noah, Ād, Thamūd, Lot, and Shuayb. For each prophet, He mentions how He destroyed those who denied him and saved him and his followers. He then concludes the account with His words:

“Surely in that is a sign, yet most of them were not believers. And indeed, your Lord is the Almighty, the Most Merciful.”

Thus He concludes the account with two of His names appropriate to what has been described: “And He is the Almighty, the Most Merciful.” Through His might He exacted retribution upon His enemies, and through His mercy He saved His messengers and their followers.

---

## Page 9616

Section

The revealed message is indispensable to the well-being of the servant in this life and the Hereafter. Just as he cannot prosper in the Hereafter except by following the revealed message, neither can he prosper in his livelihood and worldly life except by following it. Human beings cannot do without divine law, for their actions are of two kinds: actions by which they obtain what benefits them, and actions by which they ward off what harms them.

Divine law is the light that makes clear what benefits them and what harms them. It is Allah’s light upon His earth, His justice among His servants, and His fortress, within which whoever enters is safe. What is meant here is not distinguishing through the senses between what is harmful and what is beneficial, for even animals that cannot speak possess this ability: a donkey or a camel can thereby distinguish barley from dirt.

Rather, it concerns discerning the harmful and beneficial effects of deeds upon those who perform them in this life and the Hereafter—for example, the benefit of faith and affirming Allah’s oneness; justice, righteousness, charity, and doing good; trustworthiness and chastity; courage and forbearance; patience, enjoining right and forbidding wrong, maintaining ties of kinship, honouring one’s parents, treating slaves and neighbours kindly, and fulfilling others’ rights; acting sincerely for Allah alone, relying upon Him, seeking His help, and being content with what He decrees for one; submitting to His judgment and obeying His command; loyalty to His allies and enmity towards His enemies;

---

## Page 9617

fearing Him in private and in public; being mindful of Him by fulfilling His obligations and avoiding His prohibitions; seeking His reward; believing Him and His Messengers in everything they have conveyed; and obeying Him in all His commands—all of which bring benefit and well-being to the servant in this life and the Hereafter. Their opposites bring him misery and harm in this life and the Hereafter.

Without the revealed message, reason would not have discerned the details of what benefits and harms people in their worldly lives and in the Hereafter. Among Allah’s greatest blessings and noblest favours upon His servants is that He sent them His Messengers, revealed His Books to them, and made the straight path clear to them. Without this, they would have been like livestock and beasts—indeed, worse off than them. Whoever accepts Allah’s message and remains steadfast upon it is among the best of creation. Whoever rejects it and departs from it is among the worst of creation, worse off than dogs, pigs, and beasts.

In the Sahīh, Abū Mūsā, may Allah be pleased with him, narrated that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The guidance and knowledge with which Allah has sent me are like rain that fell upon a land. One part of it absorbed the water and brought forth abundant pasture and grass. Another part was barren ground that retained the water, through which Allah benefited people: they drank from it, made use of it, and cultivated their crops. The rain also fell upon another part consisting only of level plains that neither retained water nor produced pasture. Such is the likeness of one who gains understanding of the religion of Allah Most High and benefits from that with which Allah has sent me, learning and teaching; and the likeness of one who pays it no heed and does not accept Allah’s guidance with which I have been sent.”

Its authenticity is agreed upon.

---

## Page 9618

So praise be to Allah, who sent us a Messenger from among ourselves, reciting Allah’s verses to us, purifying us, and teaching us the Book and wisdom, although before that we were clearly astray. The people of Paradise said:

“Praise be to Allah, who has guided us to this. We would never have been guided had Allah not guided us. The Messengers of our Lord certainly came with the truth.”

The whole world is accursed, and everything in it is accursed, except that upon which the sun of the divine message has shone and which is built upon that message. The inhabitants of the earth can endure only as long as traces of the Messengers remain among them. When those traces have faded from the earth and been completely effaced, Allah will destroy the upper and lower worlds and bring about the Resurrection.

The need of the earth’s inhabitants for the Messenger is not like their need for the sun and moon, the winds and rain; nor is it like a person’s need for life, the eye’s need for its light, or the body’s need for food and drink. Rather, it is greater and more pressing than any need one could conceive or imagine. The Messengers are intermediaries between Allah and His creation in conveying His commands and prohibitions; they are His envoys to His servants.

Their seal and leader, and the most honoured of them before his Lord, Muhammad ibn ‘Abd Allāh, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to say:

“O people, I am but a mercy bestowed as a gift.”

Allah Most High said:

“We have not sent you except as a mercy to the worlds.”

And he, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him, said:

“Allah looked upon the inhabitants of the earth and detested them, Arabs and non-Arabs alike, except for some remnants of the People of the Book.”

This detestation was because they had not been guided by the Messengers.

---

## Page 9619

so Allah lifted this detestation from them through the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He sent him as a mercy to the worlds, a clear path for seekers, and a proof against all creation. He made it obligatory upon His servants to obey him, love him, support him, honour him, and fulfil the duties owed to him. He closed every path to Himself except the path of His Messenger. He took pledges and solemn covenants from all the Prophets and Messengers to believe in him and follow him, and commanded them to take those same covenants from the believers who followed them.

Allah sent him with guidance and the religion of truth before the coming of the Hour, as a bearer of glad tidings and a warner, one who calls to Allah by His permission, and an illuminating lamp. Through him He brought the succession of Messengers to its close, guided people out of misguidance, and taught them what they did not know. Through his message He opened blind eyes, deaf ears, and closed hearts. Through his message the earth shone after its darkness, and hearts were united after their division. Through it He set right the religion that had become distorted and made the radiant path clear. He opened his heart, relieved him of his burden, exalted his renown, and imposed humiliation and abasement upon those who opposed his command.

He sent him after an interval without Messengers, when the scriptures had faded away, words had been distorted, and revealed laws had been altered. Every people relied upon their darkest opinions, making judgments concerning Allah and among His servants according to their corrupt doctrines and desires. Through him Allah guided creation, made the path clear, brought people out of darkness into light, gave them sight after blindness, and guided them out of error. He made him the dividing line between Paradise and Hell, distinguished the righteous from the wicked, and made guidance and success lie in following him and conforming to his way

---

## Page 9620

and misguidance and misery in disobeying him and opposing his way. Through him Allah tests people in their graves: they are questioned about him and tested concerning him.

“The servant is approached in his grave and asked, ‘What did you use to say about this man who was sent among you?’

“The believer says, ‘I bear witness that he is Allah’s servant. He brought us clear proofs and guidance, so we believed in him and followed him.’ He is told, ‘You have spoken the truth. Upon this you lived, upon it you died, and upon it you will be resurrected, if Allah wills. Sleep like a bridegroom whom none but his dearest family member awakens.’ Then his grave is made spacious and illuminated for him, and a door to Paradise is opened for him, increasing his gladness and joy.

“As for the disbeliever and the hypocrite, each says, ‘I do not know. I heard people saying something, so I repeated it.’ He is told, ‘We already knew that. Upon that you lived, upon it you died, and upon it you will be resurrected, if Allah wills.’ Then he is struck with an iron sledgehammer and lets out a scream that everything except human beings hears.”

Allah has commanded obedience to His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in more than thirty places in the Quran. He has linked obedience to His Messenger with obedience to Himself, and disobedience to His Messenger with disobedience to Himself, just as He has linked His own name with his name. Thus Allah is not mentioned without His Messenger being mentioned alongside Him. Ibn ʿAbbās said

---

## Page 9621

—may Allah be pleased with him—concerning the Most High’s words,

“And We raised high your renown”:

“I am not mentioned without you being mentioned alongside Me.” This is exemplified in the testimony of faith recited in prayer, sermons, and the call to prayer: “I bear witness that none has the right to be worshipped except Allah, and I bear witness that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.” Thus, a person’s Islam is not valid without mentioning him and testifying to his messengership. Likewise, neither the call to prayer, the prayer itself, nor the sermon is valid without mentioning him and testifying to his messengership.

Allah, glorified and exalted be He, has warned those who oppose him of punishment and disbelief. The Most High says:

“Do not treat the Messenger’s call among you like your calls to one another. Allah certainly knows those of you who slip away, taking cover behind others. So let those who oppose his command beware lest a trial befall them or a painful punishment afflict them.”

Imam Ahmad—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—said: “What trial is this? It is none other than disbelief.”

Likewise, Allah, glorified be He, has inflicted humiliation and abasement upon those who oppose his command, as related in the Musnad of Imam Ahmad, in the hadith of ‘Abdullāh ibn ‘Umar, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“I have been sent shortly before the Hour so that Allah alone may be worshipped, without partner. My provision has been placed beneath the shade of my spear, and humiliation and abasement have been laid upon those who oppose my command. Whoever imitates a people is one of them.”

---

## Page 9622

And just as whoever disobeys him, opposes him, and shows enmity towards him is wretched and doomed, so too is whoever turns away from him and what he brought, finds reassurance in something else, and accepts it in his stead.

Wretchedness and misguidance lie in turning away from him and denying him, while guidance and success lie in embracing what he brought and giving it precedence over everything else. People thus fall into three categories: those who believe in him—that is, those who follow him, love him, and give him precedence over others; those who are hostile to him and openly oppose him; and those who turn away from what he brought. The first are blessed, while the other two are doomed.

We therefore ask Allah the Almighty to make us among those who follow him and believe in him, to let us live according to his Sunnah and die adhering to it, and never to separate us from it. He indeed hears supplication and is worthy of our hope. He is sufficient for us, and He is the best Trustee. All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds. May Allah bless our master Muhammad, his family, and his good and pure Companions.

---

## Page 9623

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—said:

Section:

On the unity of faith and the plurality and diversity of revealed laws; the unity of religion in respect of faith, but not of law; the retention and abrogation this involves; and how this also applies, in a certain respect, to the followers of a single revealed law.

Allah Most High said:

“And when his Lord tested Abraham with commands, which he fulfilled, He said, ‘I will make you a leader for mankind.’”

This explicitly states that he is a leader for all mankind. He also said:

“Indeed, Abraham was a model.”

That is, an exemplar to be followed and a teacher of goodness. He also said:

“And who would turn away from the faith of Abraham except one who is foolish at heart? We certainly chose him in this world, and in the Hereafter he will surely be among the righteous.”

“When his Lord said to him, ‘Submit,’ he said, ‘I have submitted to the Lord of all worlds.’”

“Abraham enjoined this upon his sons, as did Jacob: ‘My sons, Allah has chosen this religion for you, so do not die except as Muslims.’”

“Or were you witnesses when death approached Jacob, when he said to his sons, ‘What will you worship after me?’ They said, ‘We will worship your God and the God of your fathers

---

## Page 9624

Abraham, Ishmael, and Isaac—the one God—and to Him we submit.’”

“That was a community that has passed away. Theirs is what they earned, and yours is what you earn; you will not be questioned about what they used to do.”

He has thus made clear that none turns away from the faith of Abraham except someone foolish, and that Abraham was commanded to submit, whereupon he said:

“I have submitted to the Lord of all worlds.”

This was Abraham’s counsel to his sons, and Israel’s counsel to his sons. Allah chose Adam, Noah, the family of Abraham, and the family of ʿImrān above all people.

Then He said:

“They say, ‘Become Jews or Christians, and you will be guided.’ Say, ‘Rather, we follow the faith of Abraham, who inclined to the truth and was not one of those who associated others with Allah.’”

He thus commanded adherence to the faith of Abraham and forbade embracing Judaism or Christianity. He commanded comprehensive faith, in accordance with what He revealed to the prophets and what they were given; submission to Him; that we be imbued with Allah’s colouring; and that we worship Him. He also refuted those who claimed that Abraham and his sons, and Israel and his sons, were Jews or Christians. Before this, He had said:

“Never will the Jews or the Christians be pleased with you until you follow their religion. Say, ‘Allah’s guidance is the true guidance.’ If you were to follow their desires…”

—to the end of the verse. The meaning is: the Jews will never be pleased with you until you follow their religion, nor will the Christians until you follow their religion. This may be cited as evidence that each community has a religion of its own, on the basis of His words, Most High:

---

## Page 9625

“The Jews say, ‘The Christians have no ground to stand on,’ and the Christians say, ‘The Jews have no ground to stand on.’”

At the end of the surah, He, Most High, said:

“The Messenger believes in what has been revealed to him from his Lord,”

—and so on to the end of the surah, just as He said at its beginning:

“And those who believe in what has been revealed to you and what was revealed before you, and who are certain of the Hereafter.”

Thus, He opened it with comprehensive faith, concluded it with comprehensive faith, and placed comprehensive faith at its centre. Our Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was granted opening and concluding words, and words of comprehensive meaning.

In Surah Āl ʿImrān, after recounting the affairs of the Messiah and John, He, Most High, said:

“Say, ‘O People of the Scripture, come to a word common to us and you: that we worship none but Allah, associate nothing with Him, and do not take one another as lords besides Allah.’ If they turn away, then say, ‘Bear witness that we are Muslims.’”

This is the verse that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, wrote to Heraclius, ruler of the Byzantines, when he called them to Islam. Allah also said:

“O People of the Scripture, why do you dispute about Abraham when the Torah and the Gospel were not revealed until after him? Will you not reason?

“Here you are, disputing about matters of which you have knowledge. Why, then, do you dispute about matters of which you have no knowledge? Allah knows, and you do not know.

“Abraham was neither a Jew nor a Christian, but one who inclined to the truth, submitting to Allah; he was not one of those who associated others with Allah.

“Indeed, those with the strongest claim to Abraham are those who followed him, this Prophet, and those who believe. Allah is the Protector of the believers.”

—up to His words: “And when Allah took the covenant of

the prophets: ‘Whatever Scripture and wisdom I have given you’”—up to His words:

“Allah bears witness that there is no deity except Him, as do the angels and those endowed with knowledge. He upholds justice. There is no deity except Him, the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

He thus declared that the religion in the sight of Allah is Islam, and that those among the People of the Scripture who differed and divided into various religions differed only after knowledge had come to them. This makes clear that the religion is one, with no division within it.

—and so on to the end of the verse.

## Page 9628

And this is mentioned in many places in the Quran.

Likewise, it appears in authentic hadiths. For example, al-Bukhārī entitled a chapter:

“Chapter: Reports That the Religion of the Prophets Is One.”

He cited the hadith recorded by both al-Bukhārī and Muslim, narrated by Abū Hurayrah from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“We prophets are brothers with one father and different mothers.”

Another example is his description in the Torah:

“I will not take him until I straighten through him the crooked faith; so open through him blind eyes, deaf ears, and veiled hearts.”

For this reason, Allah uses the singular for the path and the way, as in His words, Exalted is He:

“Guide us to the straight path—the path of those You have blessed, not those who have incurred Your anger, nor those who have gone astray.”

And His words:

“This is My path, perfectly straight, so follow it, and do not follow other ways.”

And His words:

“Allah is the Protector of those who believe. He brings them out of darkness into light.”

And His words:

“The example of those who spend their wealth in the way of Allah.”

“And strove in the way of Allah.”

And His words:

“Fight them until there is no more persecution and religion is entirely for Allah.”

Islam is the religion of all the messengers. Noah, peace be upon him, said:

“But if you turn away, I have asked you for no reward. My reward is only with Allah, and I have been commanded to be one of the Muslims.”

Allah said concerning Abraham and his sons what has already been mentioned. Allah also related the words of the sorcerers:

“Our Lord, pour patience upon us and cause us to die as Muslims.”

And those of Pharaoh:

“I believe that there is no deity except the One in whom the Children of Israel believe, and I am one of the Muslims.”

Shaykh al-Islām said:

Elsewhere, I have explained Islam in its general and specific senses, and faith in its general and specific senses, as in His words:

“Indeed, those who believe, the Jews, the Christians, and the Sabians—whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day and does righteous deeds—will have their reward with their Lord. No fear will be upon them, nor will they grieve.”

As for the diversity and multiplicity of revealed laws, Allah, Exalted is He, mentioned the direction of prayer after mentioning the faith, saying:

“So turn your face toward the Sacred Mosque. Wherever you are, turn your faces toward it. Those who were given the Scripture certainly know that it is the truth from their Lord. And Allah is not unaware of what they do.”

Then, further on, He says:

“Each has a direction toward which he turns, so compete in doing good.”

He thus states that every community has a direction of prayer. He did not say, ‘We have appointed a direction of prayer for every community.’ Rather, they may have invented it themselves, just as the Christians invented the practice of facing east. This differs from what He says concerning religious law and prescribed paths, for He says:

“O Messenger, do not let those who hasten into disbelief grieve you.”

And so on, up to His words:

---

## Page 9630

“And who is better than Allah in judgment for a people who are certain?”

These verses were revealed concerning judgments involving prescribed punishments, retaliation, and blood money. He stated that the Torah was that:

“By which the prophets who submitted to Allah judged for the Jews, as did the devout scholars and rabbis, by virtue of what they were entrusted to preserve.”

This applies generally to all the prophets, devout scholars, and rabbis. Then, when He mentioned the Gospel, He said:

“Let the people of the Gospel judge by what Allah has revealed therein.”

He commanded these people to judge accordingly because the Gospel contains some of what is in the Torah and confirms most of it. Judging by what Allah revealed in it is therefore also judging by what is in the Torah. Then He said:

“So judge between them by what Allah has revealed, and do not follow their desires, turning away from the truth that has come to you. To each of you We appointed a law and a clear path.”

He thus commanded him to judge by what Allah had revealed to those before him. “To each We appointed”—of the two messengers and the two scriptures—“a law and a clear path,” meaning a prescribed practice and a way. The law is the revealed law, namely the prescribed practice; the clear path is the road or way. This explained why he was to leave the practice and path appointed for others in favor of what had been appointed for him.

Then He commanded him to judge between them by what Allah had revealed to him. The first forbids him to adopt another’s path and law. The second—even if it constitutes a ruling other than the ruling that was revealed—forbids him to abandon anything revealed therein concerning following Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, whom they find mentioned in writing in the Torah and the Gospel that they possess. Whoever does not follow him has not judged by what Allah revealed, even if he is not among the People of the Scripture who were commanded to judge by what it contains of provisions differing from his judgment.

---

## Page 9631

Allah Most High also said in Sūrat al-Hajj:

“For every community We have appointed a rite, so that they may mention Allah’s name over the livestock He has provided for them.”

And:

“For every community We have appointed a rite which they observe, so let them not dispute with you concerning the matter.”

He also said within the surah:

“Monasteries, churches, synagogues, and mosques in which Allah’s name is frequently mentioned would surely have been demolished.”

He thus made clear that He Himself appointed the rites, and He mentioned the places of worship, just as He mentioned in Sūrat al-Baqarah the direction towards which they turn. In Sūrat al-Jāthiyah, after mentioning the Children of Israel, He said:

“Then We set you upon a prescribed way concerning the matter, so follow it, and do not follow the desires of those who do not know”—to the end of the verse.

Concerning abrogation and their obligation to follow the Messenger, He said:

“And when Allah took the covenant of the prophets: ‘Whatever I give you of Scripture and wisdom’”—through to His words: “And I am with you among the witnesses.”

He also said:

“I shall decree it for those who fear Me and give zakah”—this verse and the one that follows it.

The passages in Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat Āl ʿImrān commanding them to believe in what Allah revealed to Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, have already been discussed. The same is found in Sūrat al-Nisāʾ, and it occurs frequently throughout the Quran.

Section

Allah Most High said to us:

“O you who believe, fear Allah as He should be feared, and do not die except as Muslims.”

“And hold firmly to the rope of Allah all together, and do not become divided

---

## Page 9632

and remember Allah’s favor upon you: you were enemies, and He united your hearts, so that by His favor you became brothers”—through to His words:

“And do not be like those who became divided and differed after clear proofs had come to them”—through to His words:

“You are the best community brought forth for mankind.”

Thus He commanded us to remain steadfast in Islam until death, just as He commanded all the prophets to submit to Him, and to hold firmly to His rope all together without becoming divided. He forbade us to be like those who became divided and differed after clear proofs had come to them. He mentioned that some faces would become white and others black. Ibn ʿAbbās said: “The faces of the people of the Sunnah and the community will become white, and the faces of the people of innovation and division will become black.” He mentioned that they would be told:

“Did you disbelieve after having believed?”

This refers back to His words:

“And do not die except as Muslims.”

He thus commanded steadfast adherence to Islam and explained that those whose faces become black—the people of division and disagreement—will be asked: “Did you disbelieve after having believed?” This is evidence of their disbelief and apostasy. The Companions applied this verse to the Khawārij.

This parallels His command to the messengers:

“Establish the religion and do not become divided over it.”

He also said in Sūrat al-Baqarah:

“Mankind was one community. Then Allah sent the prophets as bearers of good tidings and warners, and sent down with them the Scripture in truth to judge between people concerning that over which they differed”—the verse.

He also said:

“Indeed, those who divided their religion and became sects—you have nothing to do with them.”

And Allah Most High said:

“But they divided their religion among themselves into sects, each faction in what

---

## Page 9633

it has, rejoicing.” And Allah Most High said:

“And direct your face toward the religion, inclining to truth, and never be among the polytheists.”

“Among those who divided their religion and became sects, each faction rejoicing in what it has.”

And He Most High said:

“Indeed, the religion in the sight of Allah is Islam. Those who were given the Scripture did not differ until after knowledge had come to them, out of mutual envy”—the verse.

“Those who were given the Scripture did not become divided until after the clear proof had come to them”—the verse. There is a similar passage in Sūrat al-Jāthiyah.

And Allah Most High said:

“O you who believe, obey Allah and obey the Messenger and those in authority among you. If you disagree over anything, refer it to Allah and the Messenger, if you believe in Allah and the Last Day. That is best and leads to the best outcome.”

And He Most High said:

“And those who came after them say, ‘Our Lord, forgive us and our brothers who preceded us in faith, and leave no resentment in our hearts toward those who believe. Our Lord, You are indeed Most Kind, Most Merciful.’”

Section

If Allah Most High has commanded us to obey Him, His Messenger, and those in authority among us; has commanded us, whenever we disagree over anything, to refer it to Allah and the Messenger; has commanded us to maintain unity and harmony and forbidden us to divide and differ; and has commanded us

---

## Page 9634

to seek forgiveness for those who preceded us in faith, has named us Muslims, and has commanded us to remain so until death, then these texts and others of similar meaning require us to be united in religion, just as the prophets before us were united in religion.

Those in authority among us are the Messenger’s successors. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said in an authentic hadith:

“The Children of Israel were governed by the prophets: whenever a prophet died, another prophet arose. There will be no prophet after me, but there will be caliphs, and they will be numerous.” They asked, “What do you command us to do, O Messenger of Allah?” He replied, “Fulfil your pledge of allegiance to the first, then the next, and give them their due, for Allah will question them about those He entrusted to their care.”

He also said:

“The scholars are the heirs of the prophets.”

It is also reported that he said:

“I wish I could see my successors.” They asked, “Who are your successors?” He replied, “Those who revive my Sunnah and teach it to the people.”

These, then, are those in authority after him: the rulers and the scholars. This is how the early generations and the imams who followed them, such as Imam Ahmad and others, explained the expression. Its meaning is clear, and we have established it elsewhere.

The fundamental principles established by the Quran, the Sunnah, and scholarly consensus are comparable to the religion shared by the prophets: no one may depart from them. Whoever embraces them belongs to the people of pure Islam, who are Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā'ah (the people of the Sunnah and the united community).

The diversity among them in prescribed deeds and sayings is comparable to the diversity among the prophets. Allah Most High said:

“And those who strive for Our sake—We will surely guide them to Our ways.”

And He Most High said:

“Indeed,

---

## Page 9635

there has come to you from Allah a light and a clear Book.”

“Through it Allah guides those who seek His pleasure to the ways of peace.”

And He said:

“O you who believe, enter into Islam completely.”

This diversity may concern obligations in some cases and recommended acts in others. An example of the former is that some people are obliged to undertake jihad, others to pay zakat, and others to teach religious knowledge. This occurs in both individual and collective obligations.

Individual obligations include each man’s duty to establish the congregational and Friday prayers where he lives, together with the people of his locality; to pay the zakat due on the kind of wealth he possesses, distributing it to eligible recipients in the vicinity of his property; to face the Kaaba from his own location; and to make the pilgrimage to Allah’s House by the route available to him. He must also honour his parents, maintain his ties of kinship, and treat his neighbours, companions, slaves, and those under his authority well. These and similar duties vary in their particular objects, although the community shares the same general obligation. At other times, obligations vary according to ability and inability, as with the differing forms of prayer for residents and travellers, the healthy and the sick, and those in safety and those in fear.

Collective obligations vary in the same ways as individual obligations, but they also have a distinctive form of variation: they become individually binding upon someone when no one else fulfils them. Thus, an obligation may become individually binding at one time and place upon a particular person or group, and at another time or place upon a different person or group. This occurs, for example, in holding positions of authority, undertaking jihad, issuing fatwas, adjudicating disputes, and other such matters.

---

## Page 9636

As for recommended acts, this diversity is even greater. Every kind of variation found in obligations also occurs in recommended acts, with an additional consideration: for each individual, the recommended acts of drawing near to Allah Most High are only those that he can perform, does perform, and benefits from. It is concerning these acts that Allah says:

“My servant continues to draw near to Me through voluntary acts until I love him.”

The best deeds for a person are those most beneficial to him, and this varies enormously. For most people, what is recommended is not what is best in absolute terms. Most cannot undertake what is best, or cannot persevere in it even if they are capable of it. They may derive no benefit from it; indeed, pursuing it may harm them. For example, someone unable to understand intricate matters of knowledge may damage his intellect and religion by pursuing them. Likewise, someone may be unable to endure the bitterness of poverty and unable to exercise restraint amid the sweetness of wealth, or unable to protect himself from the temptations of authority and persevere in fulfilling its duties.

For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, relating from his Lord, Almighty and Majestic:

“Among My servants are those whom nothing but poverty keeps upright; were I to make them wealthy, that would corrupt them. And among My servants are those whom nothing but wealth keeps upright; were I to make them poor, that would corrupt them.”

When Abū Dharr asked him for a position of authority, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“O Abū Dharr, I consider you weak, and I love for you what I love for myself. Do not take authority over even two people, nor take charge of an orphan’s property.”

It is also reported that he said to his uncle al-ʿAbbās:

“Saving your own soul is better than holding an office whose responsibilities you cannot fulfil.”

Thus, when we say, “This deed is better,” this is an unqualified statement.

---

## Page 9637

Yet a less excellent deed may be better in its proper setting, or better for someone for whom the more excellent deed is unsuitable. For example, reciting the Quran is superior to remembrance of Allah, as established by the revealed texts, scholarly consensus, and reasoned consideration.

As for the revealed texts, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The best words after the Quran are four phrases—and they are also found in the Quran: Glory be to Allah; praise be to Allah; there is no god but Allah; and Allah is the Greatest.”

He also said:

“The superiority of the Quran over all other speech is like Allah’s superiority over His creation.”

He said, relating from Allah:

“Whoever is so occupied with reciting the Quran that he is kept from remembering Me and asking of Me, I will give him better than what I give those who ask.”

He also said:

“The servants have never drawn near to Allah through anything like that which has come from Him.”

A Bedouin said to him, “I am unable to learn any of the Quran, so teach me something that will suffice in my prayer.” He replied, “Say: Glory be to Allah; praise be to Allah; there is no god but Allah; and Allah is the Greatest.”

As for consensus on this, a number of scholars have reported it, and the dissent of ignorant devotees carries no weight.

As for reasoned consideration, recitation is obligatory in prayer. If a person is unable to recite, he turns instead to remembrance of Allah. Remembrance does not suffice when he is able to recite. The original act is superior to a substitute that is permitted only when one is unable to perform the original.

---

## Page 9638

Moreover, recitation requires purification from major ritual impurity, just as prayer requires purification from both major and minor ritual impurity, whereas remembrance requires neither. Thus, the highest form of remembering Allah is prayer, followed by recitation, then remembrance in general. Yet during bowing and prostration, remembrance is superior to reciting the Quran, according to the revealed texts and scholarly consensus.

Likewise, many worshippers may initially benefit from remembrance in ways they do not benefit from recitation. Remembrance imparts faith, whereas the Quran imparts knowledge; a person may not understand it and, being at the outset, may need faith more than knowledge. For people of faith, however, reciting the Quran with understanding is superior by agreement.

This and similar examples resemble the diversity of the laws prescribed for the prophets. They all agree that Allah commanded each of them to follow the religion common to them all, and that we should worship Him according to that prescribed law and way. Likewise, the Muslim community agrees that Allah has commanded every Muslim to observe what the law of the Quran prescribes for him, whether as an obligation or a recommendation, even though the actions prescribed may differ for different groups within the community. Their beliefs do not differ, nor does the One they worship, and none of them is mistaken. Rather, they all agree on this, each affirming the truth of the others.

---

## Page 9639

Section

As for what resembles this in one respect but not another, it consists of disputed matters in which those concerned were allowed to maintain their respective positions and act upon them, based on the independent judgment of scholars, shaykhs, commanders, and kings. Examples include the Companions’ differing judgments concerning cutting down date palms or leaving them standing, and their judgments concerning the afternoon prayer when the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, sent them to Banū Qurayza and commanded them not to pray it until they reached Banū Qurayza. Some prayed along the way, within its appointed time, saying, “He only intended us to hasten, not to miss the prayer.” Others delayed it until they arrived and prayed after its appointed time, adhering to the apparent generality of his wording. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, rebuked neither group.

He, peace and blessings be upon him, also said:

“When a judge exercises independent judgment and reaches the correct decision, he receives two rewards; when he exercises independent judgment and errs, he receives one reward.”

In matters over which the Companions disagreed, they agreed that each group should allow the other to act according to its own independent judgment. These included matters of worship, marriage, inheritance, stipends, governance, and other issues. In the first year, ‘Umar ruled in the Himāriyya inheritance case against allowing full siblings to share with maternal half-siblings; in the following year, he allowed them to share in a case identical to the first. When asked about this, he said: “That case stands according to what we ruled then, and this one according to what we rule now.”

---

## Page 9640

And they are the leaders whom the revealed texts establish will never agree upon falsehood or misguidance, and whom the Quran and Sunnah require us to follow. They also disagreed over questions of knowledge and belief, such as whether the dead hear the voices of the living, whether the deceased is punished because his family weeps over him, and whether Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, saw his Lord before his death, while maintaining their unity and mutual affection.

Among these questions are some in which one of the two positions is certainly wrong. In others, according to the majority who follow the early generations, only one disputant is correct in reality, while the other has fulfilled his duty according to his powers of understanding. Whether the latter should be called correct or mistaken is disputed. Some people consider all the disputants correct and hold that there is no objective ruling on the matter. The position of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah is that whoever exercises independent judgment incurs no sin, even if he errs.

This type therefore resembles the first type in one respect but differs from it in another. The difference is that the prophets, peace be upon them, are protected from being allowed to persist in error, unlike individual scholars and rulers, who have no such protection. Thus, it is permissible—indeed, obligatory—to explain the truth that must be followed, even when doing so entails exposing an error committed by a scholar or ruler. As for the prophets, neither sets forth anything that exposes the other as mistaken.

The resemblance lies in the fact that each is commanded to follow whatever truth has become clear to him through scriptural evidence, just as the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, was commanded to follow what was revealed to him.

---

## Page 9641

and neither may require the other to obey him, just as neither prophet may require this of the other. Evidence previously hidden from him may become apparent, leading him, through independent judgment, to abandon an earlier judgment. This resembles abrogation in the case of a prophet; however, the former sets aside a belief, whereas the latter actually repeals a ruling.

Followers must follow those rulers and scholars entrusted with their affairs in matters where such following is permissible and where following their independent judgment is commanded, just as a community must follow whichever prophet is sent to them, even if his law differs from that of the preceding prophet. Yet the variation and change in the law as it applies to these people does not arise from any variation in the command itself revealed to the Messenger, but from differences in their circumstances. One apprehends the revelation that reaches him, both by hearing it and by understanding it, while another is unable to apprehend that message: either he cannot hear it because he has no opportunity to hear that scriptural text, or he cannot understand it because he does not grasp what the first understood from the text. When he is incapable, he bears no sin for what lies beyond his ability. One may recognize the other’s inability and error and excuse him for it, or he may not recognize his inability. Neither may be able to determine which of them has apprehended the truth and reached the correct conclusion.

For this reason, some have declined to call such a case an error, arguing: “Religious obligation is conditional upon ability. Whatever knowledge a person was incapable of attaining did not constitute Allah’s ruling in his case, so he cannot be said to have missed it.”

The majority, however, say that he has missed it, as indicated by the Sunnah and scholarly consensus, but that his error is excused. This is what is meant by the statement that he was unable to apprehend and know it. Yet

---

## Page 9642

this does not preclude that ruling from being what Allah intended and commanded. A person’s inability to understand a scholar’s words does not preclude the scholar from having intended that meaning, nor does it preclude the person who understood it from being correct and receiving two rewards.

For this reason, the scholars of our school have differed concerning someone who fails to arrive at the true ruling: may he be described as outwardly correct because he fulfilled the duty within his power by exercising independent judgment and confining himself to what he could accomplish? Or should he never be described as correct, even though he is rewarded for exercising independent judgment and seeking the truth? These are two views, both reported from Ahmad. Such a person has not arrived at the true ruling, but he has sought the truth. Yet has he exercised the independent judgment he was commanded to exercise?

The precise conclusion is that he has exercised independent judgment to the extent of his ability. He is therefore correct in this respect: he has fulfilled what he was commanded to do within his capacity, even though he has not been correct in attaining the intended result and performing what is commanded in absolute terms.

This becomes clear when we recognize that authority is of two kinds. The first is the authority of proof and knowledge; this is what the Quran most often calls authority. Indeed, it is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās that every occurrence of authority in the Quran means proof. The second is the authority of power. Righteous action cannot be accomplished without both forms of authority. When the authority of proof is weak, the obligation is proportionate to it; when the authority of power is weak, the obligation is likewise proportionate to it. Obligation is conditional upon capacity in both respects, so inability in either respect removes liability to sin for failing to fulfil the command.

Allah’s authority in matters of knowledge is the prophetic message, which is His proof against His creation, as He, Exalted is He, says:

---

## Page 9643

“so that people will have no argument against Allah after the messengers.”

And He, Exalted is He, says:

“They are nothing but names you and your forefathers have named, for which Allah has sent down no authority.”

And He says:

“Or have We sent down to them an authority that speaks in support of what they associate with Him?”

There are numerous similar passages.

The schools of thought, paths, and policies adopted by scholars, shaykhs, and rulers are, in certain respects, comparable for them to the laws and ways prescribed for the prophets—provided that through them they seek the countenance of Allah Most High, rather than pursuing their desires; that they seek to hold fast to the all-embracing faith and religion, which consists in worshipping Allah alone, without partner; and that, after exercising their utmost judgment, they follow to the best of their ability what has been sent down to them from their Lord in the Book and the Sunnah.

They are rewarded for seeking Allah’s countenance and worshipping Him alone, without partner—this being the fundamental, all-embracing religion—just as the prophets are rewarded for worshipping Allah alone, without partner. They are also rewarded for obeying Allah and His Messenger in what they hold to—not from His Messenger’s law and way—just as every prophet is rewarded for obeying Allah in his own law and way.

Their laws and ways vary. For example, one of them may transmit hadiths in wording different from that which reached another; some Quranic verses may be explained to him in wording different from that of another explanation; and, in reconciling the texts and deriving rulings from them, he may employ a method of arrangement and harmonization different from the method

---

## Page 9644

followed by another. The same applies to his acts of worship and ways of turning to Allah. One may rely on a particular verse or hadith, while another relies on a different hadith or verse. The same applies to knowledge. Some scholars follow the method of a particular scholar, which then becomes their law, until they hear another scholar’s words and see his method; they then give preference to whichever of the two is stronger. Thus, the statements and actions mentioned above vary in their case in this respect. They are commanded to uphold the religion and not divide within it, just as the messengers were commanded. They are also commanded not to divide the community, for it is one community, just as the messengers were commanded. This obligation is all the more binding upon them, since they are united by one revealed law and one Book.

As for the matters over which they disagree, it cannot be said that Allah has commanded each of them to adhere inwardly and outwardly to his own position, as the prophets were commanded to do—even though a group of speculative theologians holds this view. Rather, what must be said is that Allah has commanded each of them to seek the truth to the utmost of his capacity and ability. If he attains it, well and good; otherwise, Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity. The believers have said:

“Our Lord, do not hold us accountable if we forget or make a mistake.”

And Allah said: “I have done so.” He, Exalted is He, also says:

“There is no blame upon you for the mistakes you make.”

Whoever condemns and blames them for something for which Allah has not held them accountable has transgressed. And whoever seeks to place their statements and actions on the same level as the words and deeds of one who is infallible, and champions them without guidance from Allah, has transgressed and followed his own desires without guidance

---

## Page 9645

from Allah. Whoever does what he has been commanded according to his circumstances—exercising independent judgment to the extent he is able, or following scholarly authority when unable to exercise independent judgment, while being fair in doing so—follows a balanced course. For commands are conditional upon ability:

“Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity.”

Thus, in every situation, a Muslim must submit himself to Allah while doing good, and remain steadfast in this submission. Submitting himself means being sincere to Allah, and doing good means performing good deeds. Reflect on this, for it is a comprehensive, beneficial, and momentous principle.

---

## Page 9646

Shaykh al-Islām said:

This is “a momentous, comprehensive principle with many ramifications.” People are greatly confused about its details, to the point that some have adopted diametrically opposed positions regarding both kinds of judgments: cognitive judgments and theoretical judgments concerning concrete realities. For every instance of knowledge, belief, judgment, and speech—indeed, even love and intentions—either depends on its object and corresponds to it, or has an object that depends on it and corresponds to it. Accordingly, truth, realities, and words are divided into what exists and what is intended; what pertains to creation and what pertains to religion; what is decreed and what is prescribed, as I have explained elsewhere.

Theorists have disputed the nature of knowledge: does it depend on the object known without affecting it, being instead passive, as many speculative theologians maintain? Or does the object known depend on knowledge, with knowledge affecting it and thus being active, as many philosophers maintain?

The correct view is that knowledge is of two kinds: one dependent, the other that on which something else depends. These two characteristics generally—or always—coexist in knowledge. Thus, our knowledge of things that do not depend on our knowledge—such as our knowledge of the existence of the heavens and the earth, and likewise our knowledge of Allah, His names and attributes, His angels, His Books, His Messengers, the Last Day, the prophets, and other things:

---

## Page 9647

is dependent, passive knowledge. Our knowledge of what depends on our knowledge—such as the actions we intend to perform—is active knowledge on which its object depends, and is a cause of that object’s existence.

Likewise, Allah’s knowledge of His sacred Self follows its object without affecting it. His knowledge of His creatures, however, is knowledge on which they depend, and through it Allah created creation, as He, the Exalted, says:

“Does He who created not know, when He is the Most Subtle, the All-Aware?”

For will necessarily entails knowledge in every being that wills, just as these attributes necessarily entail life. There can be no will without knowledge, and neither will nor knowledge without life. It may, however, be permissible to say that all knowledge follows and corresponds to its object, whether or not it is a cause of that object’s existence. Thus, the speculative theologians’ unqualified formulation would be better and more accurate than the philosophers’ unqualified claim that all knowledge is active and that its object depends on it.

I can only suppose that the rational members of both groups intend a sound meaning, seeking to express what they have conceived. The former consider how knowledge follows and corresponds to its object; the latter point to an aspect of the merit of knowledge in general: it may affect its object and other things and be a cause of them, and all created things came into existence through Allah’s knowledge.

A person’s knowledge of what constitutes truth or falsehood, guidance or misguidance, sound judgment or error, truthfulness or lying, and righteousness or corruption in his beliefs, intentions, words, deeds, and the like combines both characteristics. Indeed, most knowledge—or all of it—combines these two aspects. This is why faith is speech and action: the speech and action of the heart, and the speech of the body

---

## Page 9648

and its action. Whoever knows Allah loves Him: his knowledge of Allah follows its object, while his love of Allah follows that knowledge. Whoever knows Satan hates him: his knowledge of Satan follows its object, while his hatred follows that knowledge. Likewise, knowledge in general necessarily produces some effect in the knower, whether love or something else. Even the Lord’s knowledge of His sacred Self—glory be to Him—is followed by attributes, words, and acts relating to His sacred Self.

Thus, all knowledge is followed by some state and some action; in this respect, it is something upon which other things depend, and is influential and active. All knowledge also corresponds and conforms to its object, whether that object is independent of it or owes its existence to it; in this respect, knowledge is dependent, passive, and corresponding to its object.

Although all knowledge has an effect, that effect need not be upon its object. Someone who believes in Allah, His angels, His Books, His messengers, and the Last Day, and consequently loves Allah, His angels, His prophets, and Paradise, and hates the Fire, does not thereby have knowledge that affects its object. Rather, it affects his love and desire for that object, or his hatred and aversion toward it.

Although all knowledge corresponds to its object, the object may be present in the knower’s mind and conception before it exists externally, as when a person conceives of his own words and deeds. Alternatively, it may exist externally before a person conceives of it and knows it, or without his conceiving of it at all.
